{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"9b351eeb-5b06-4fc5-a6b7-58da839e4ef5","agentId":"adeb237427453514e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"READ-ONLY deep study (no edits, no servers, no benchmarks). Study how Dragonfly implements io_uring, via the helio source on this box. Key files:\n- /shared/Projects/helio-src/util/fibers/uring_proactor.cc and .h (the core proactor event loop)\n- /shared/Projects/helio-src/util/fibers/uring_socket.cc and .h (per-connection socket ops)\n- /shared/Projects/helio-src/util/fibers/uring_types.h\n- /shared/Projects/dragonfly-src/src/facade/ (dragonfly_connection.cc, dfly_main.cc, or wherever the connection read/write loop lives — grep for Recv/Send/uring)\n\nProduce a concrete technical report answering EXACTLY these, with file:line evidence and the actual code shape (not generalities):\n1. RING SETUP: which IORING_SETUP_* flags does helio's UringProactor use (SINGLE_ISSUER? DEFER_TASKRUN? SQPOLL? COOP_TASKRUN? CQSIZE?)? Does it register the ring fd (io_uring_register_ring_fd)? How is the ring sized (sqe/cqe entries)? Any kernel-version gating/probing?\n2. SUBMIT MODEL: how are SQEs submitted — one io_uring_submit per op, or batched? Is it submit_and_wait? How does the fiber scheduler decide when to submit vs wait? (This is the core of the \"fully-exploited\" design — describe the fiber<->CQE loop precisely.)\n3. RECV PATH: multishot recv? provided-buffer rings (IORING_OP_PROVIDE_BUFFERS / buf ring / --uring_recv_buffer_cnt)? RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST? How are buffers recycled?\n4. SEND PATH: plain SEND, SENDMSG, or SEND_ZC (zero-copy)? If ZC, how do they handle the F_NOTIF completion + buffer lifetime (sync wait vs async)? registered buffers (io_uring_register_buffers)?\n5. ACCEPT / connection: multishot accept? how are new conns assigned to proactor threads?\n6. THREADING: is it strictly one ring per thread, single-issuer? How do cross-thread wakeups work (does one proactor submit to another's ring, or use eventfd/msg_ring)?\n7. Anything notable/clever we should know (linked SQEs, IOSQE_IO_LINK, fixed files, NAPI busy-poll, direct descriptors).\n\nThen a COMPARISON TABLE: helio's choice vs what our THredis deep-uring branch does (our design, from /shared/Projects/overnight_sweep/selfimprove/PLAN2.md track U + the git log of /shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during branch 2s-deep-uring-dev commits 4315accef/5c77d7b32: we do SINGLE_ISSUER|DEFER_TASKRUN with fallback probe, register_ring_fd, one batched submit_and_wait per beforeSleep pass, runtime-sized provided-buffer ring + POLL_FIRST multishot recv, detach-on-submit ZC send with async F_NOTIF reaping + registered buffer pool; recv ring stays PLAIN because on kernel 6.17 SINGLE_ISSUER+DEFER_TASKRUN makes cross-task submit + IORING_REGISTER_SYNC_CANCEL return -EINVAL which our conn-migration cancel needs). For each dimension: do we match, differ, or miss something helio does that would matter on a real NIC? Be specific about what helio does that we DON'T, and whether it's applicable to our architecture (note: we are a sharded KV with dedicated io threads + a fiber-free thread model, dragonfly is fiber-per-connection shard-per-thread). Return the report as text."},"uuid":"0f4795d5-d8e0-4c92-8cef-e380adaf390a","timestamp":"2026-07-07T12:06:27.501Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}

<string.h>\n#include <sys/eventfd.h>\n#include <sys/mman.h>\n#include <sys/syscall.h>\n\n#include <bit>\n\n#include \"base/cycle_clock.h\"\n#include \"base/flags.h\"\n#include \"base/histogram.h\"\n#include \"base/logging.h\"\n#include \"base/proc_util.h\"\n#include \"util/fibers/detail/scheduler.h\"\n#include \"util/fibers/uring_socket.h\"\n\n// We must ensure that there is no leakage of socket descriptors with enable_direct_fd enabled.\n// See AcceptServerTest.Shutdown to trigger direct fd resize.\nABSL_FLAG(uint32_t, uring_direct_table_len, 0, \"If positive create direct fd table of this length\");\nABSL_FLAG(bool, uring_disable_iowait, false, \"If true, disables iowait cpu accounting\");\n\n#define VPRO(verbosity) VLOG(verbosity) << \"PRO[\" << GetPoolIndex() << \"] \"\n\n// #define CHECK_WAKE_LATENCY 1\n\nusing namespace std;\n\n// #define DBG_EPOLL 1\nnamespace util {\n\nusing detail::SafeErrorMessage;\n\nnamespace fb2 {\n\nstatic_assert(sizeof(FiberCall) == 48);\n\nusing detail::FiberInterface;\n\nnamespace {\n\nvoid wait_for_cqe(io_uring* ring, unsigned wait_nr, __kernel_timespec* ts, sigset_t* sig = NULL) {\n  struct io_uring_cqe* cqe_ptr = nullptr;\n\n  int res = io_uring_wait_cqes(ring, &cqe_ptr, wait_nr, ts, sig);\n  if (res < 0) {\n    res = -res;\n    LOG_IF(ERROR, res != EAGAIN && res != EINTR && res != ETIME) << SafeErrorMessage(res);\n  }\n}\n\nconstexpr uint64_t kIgnoreIndex = 0;\nconstexpr uint64_t kWakeIndex = 1;\n\nconstexpr uint64_t kUserDataCbIndex = 1024;\nconstexpr uint16_t kMsgRingSubmitTag = 1;\nconstexpr uint16_t kTimeoutSubmitTag = 2;\nconstexpr uint16_t kCqeBatchLen = 128;\nconstexpr size_t kAlign = 4096;\n}  // namespace\n\nstruct UringProactor::BufRingGroup {\n  io_uring_buf_ring* ring = nullptr;\n  uint8_t* storage = nullptr;\n\n  uint32_t entry_size = 0;\n\n  // tracks the offset for incremental buffers.\n  // See https://lore.gnuweeb.org/io-uring/20240824154555.110170-1-axboe@kernel.dk/\n  uint32_t incr_offset = 0;\n\n  // Tracks the head of the ring. Updated upon each multishot completion.\n  // Relevant only for bundles.\n  uint16_t cached_head = 0;\n\n  uint8_t nentries_exp = 0;  // 2^nentries_exp is the number of entries.\n  uint16_t reserved = 0;\n\n  void AddToRing(uint16_t bid, uint16_t mask, uint16_t offset);\n\n  uint16_t size() const {\n    return 1U << nentries_exp;\n  }\n\n  const struct io_uring_buf& current_buf() const {\n    return ring->bufs[cached_head & io_uring_buf_ring_mask(size())];\n  }\n\n  void AdvanceHead() {\n    AddToRing(current_buf().bid, io_uring_buf_ring_mask(size()), 0);\n    io_uring_buf_ring_advance(ring, 1);\n    ++cached_head;\n    incr_offset = 0;\n  }\n\n  // len is a positive result passed from the callback.\n  pair<uint8_t*, uint32_t> GetBufPtr(uint16_t bid, uint32_t len);\n};\n\nvoid UringProactor::BufRingGroup::AddToRing(uint16_t bid, uint16_t mask, uint16_t offset) {\n  uint8_t* cur_buf = storage + bid * entry_size;\n  io_uring_buf_ring_add(ring, cur_buf, entry_size, bid, mask, offset);\n}\n\npair<uint8_t*, uint32_t> UringProactor::BufRingGroup::GetBufPtr(uint16_t bid, uint32_t len) {\n  DCHECK_LT(bid, size());\n\n  DCHECK_EQ(bid, current_buf().bid);\n\n  auto* start = storage + bid * entry_size + incr_offset;\n\n  // Kernel sends us total len received that can overlap over multiple buffers and might need\n  // alignment due to incr_offset. We adjust len and return the slice\n  if (len + incr_offset > entry_size) {\n    len = entry_size - incr_offset;\n  }\n  DCHECK(start + len <= storage + entry_size * size());\n  return {start, len};\n}\n\nUringProactor::UringProactor() : ProactorBase() {\n}\n\nUringProactor::~UringProactor() {\n  if (pending_epoll_removals_) {\n    LOG(DFATAL) << \"Pending epoll removals: \" << pending_epoll_removals_;\n  }\n\n  CHECK(is_stopped_);\n  if (thread_id_ != -1U) {\n    if (buf_pool_.backing) {\n      munmap(buf_pool_.backing, buf_pool_.segments.Size() * kAlign);\n      io_uring_unregister_buffers(&ring_);\n    }\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < bufring_groups_.size(); ++i) {\n      const auto& group = bufring_groups_[i];\n      if (group.ring != nullptr) {\n        io_uring_free_buf_ring(&ring_, group.ring, group.size(), i);\n        delete[] group.storage;\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (!register_fds_.empty()) {\n      io_uring_unregister_files(&ring_);\n    }\n    io_uring_queue_exit(&ring_);\n  }\n  VLOG(1) << \"Closing wake_fd \" << wake_fd_ << \" ring fd: \" << ring_.ring_fd;\n}\n\nvoid UringProactor::Init(unsigned pool_index, size_t ring_size, int wq_fd) {\n  CHECK_EQ(0U, ring_size & (ring_size - 1));\n  CHECK_GE(ring_size, 8U);\n  CHECK_EQ(0U, thread_id_) << \"Init was already called\";\n\n  pool_index_ = pool_index;\n\n  base::sys::KernelVersion kver;\n  base::sys::GetKernelVersion(&kver);\n\n  CHECK(kver.kernel > 5 || (kver.kernel == 5 && kver.major >= 8))\n      << \"Versions 5.8 or higher are supported\";\n\n  io_uring_params params;\n  memset(&params, 0, sizeof(params));\n\n  msgring_supported_f_ = 0;\n  poll_first_ = 0;\n  buf_ring_f_ = 0;\n  bundle_f_ = 0;\n  incremental_buf_ring_f_ = 0;\n  taskrun_flag_f_ = 0;\n\n  // If we setup flags that kernel does not recognize, it fails the setup call.\n  if (kver.kernel > 5 || (kver.kernel == 5 && kver.major >= 19)) {\n    params.flags |= IORING_SETUP_SUBMIT_ALL;\n    // we can notify kernel that it can skip send/receive operations and do polling first.\n    poll_first_ = 1;\n\n    // io_uring_register_buf_ring is supported since 5.19.\n    buf_ring_f_ = 1;\n\n    // FLAGS_uring_direct_table_len is a failswitch to disable direct fds.\n    register_fds_.resize(absl::GetFlag(FLAGS_uring_direct_table_len), -1);\n  }\n\n  if (kver.kernel > 6 || (kver.kernel == 6 && kver.major >= 1)) {\n    // This has a positive effect on CPU usage, latency and throughput.\n    // IORING_SETUP_TASKRUN_FLAG is required for the kernel to publish IORING_SQ_TASKRUN in\n    // sq_flags so that io_uring_submit would enter kernel to fetch completions.\n    params.flags |= (IORING_SETUP_DEFER_TASKRUN | IORING_SETUP_COOP_TASKRUN |\n                     IORING_SETUP_TASKRUN_FLAG | IORING_SETUP_SINGLE_ISSUER);\n    taskrun_flag_f_ = 1;\n  }\n\n  // Officially it's from 6.12 but we take a margin here, just in case.\n  if (kver.kernel > 6 || (kver.kernel == 6 && kver.major >= 14)) {\n    incremental_buf_ring_f_ = 1;\n  }\n\n  // it seems that SQPOLL requires registering each fd, including sockets fds.\n  // need to check if its worth pursuing.\n  // For sure not in short-term.\n  // params.flags = IORING_SETUP_SQPOLL;\n  VLOG(1) << \"Create uring of size \" << ring_size;\n\n  // If this fails with 'can not allocate memory' most probably you need to increase maxlock limit.\n  int init_res = io_uring_queue_init_params(ring_size, &ring_, &params);\n  if (init_res < 0) {\n    init_res = -init_res;\n    if (init_res == ENOMEM) {\n      LOG(ERROR)\n          << \"io_uring does not have enough memory. That can happen when your max locked \"\n             \"memory is too limited. If you run me via docker, try adding '--ulimit memlock=-1' to\"\n             \"docker run command\";\n      exit(1);\n    }\n    LOG(FATAL) << \"Error initializing io_uring: (\" << init_res << \") \"\n               << SafeErrorMessage(init_res);\n  }\n\n  io_uring_probe* uring_probe = io_uring_get_probe_ring(&ring_);\n\n  msgring_supported_f_ = io_uring_opcode_supported(uring_probe, IORING_OP_MSG_RING);\n  io_uring_free_probe(uring_probe);\n  VLOG_IF(1, msgring_supported_f_) << \"msgring supported!\";\n\n  unsigned req_feats = IORING_FEAT_SINGLE_MMAP | IORING_FEAT_FAST_POLL | IORING_FEAT_NODROP;\n  CHECK_EQ(req_feats, params.features & req_feats)\n      << \"required feature feature is not present in the kernel\";\n\n  if (params.features & IORING_FEAT_RECVSEND_BUNDLE) {\n    bundle_f_ = 1;\n  }\n\n  // io_uring_register_ring_fd \"succeeds\" on oracle linux 5.15 even though it should not\n  // as this feature is available only from 5.18. The problem is that later during shutdown\n  // io_uring_unregister_ring_fd deadlocks upon exit.\n  if (kver.kernel >= 6) {\n    int res = io_uring_register_ring_fd(&ring_);\n    LOG_IF(WARNING, res < 0) << \"io_uring_register_ring_fd failed: \" << -res;\n\n    if (absl::GetFlag(FLAGS_uring_disable_iowait)) {\n      res = io_uring_set_iowait(&ring_, false);\n      LOG_IF(WARNING, res < 0) << \"Failed to disable iowait: \" << -res;\n    }\n  }\n\n  if (!register_fds_.empty()) {\n    int res = io_uring_register_files(&ring_, register_fds_.data(), register_fds_.size());\n    CHECK_EQ(0, res);\n  }","numLines":260,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1133}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7942aa22-abad-4a98-abc5-07c590dba88d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}

return make_unexpected(std::move(ec));\n351\t}\n352\t\n353\tio::Result<size_t> UringSocket::Recv(const io::MutableBytes& mb, int flags) {\n354\t  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n355\t  Proactor* p = make_unexpected(std::move(ec));\n390\t}\n391\t\n392\tvoid UringSocket::RegisterOnErrorCb(std::function<void(uint32_t)> cb) {\n393\t  CHECK(!error_cb_wrapper_);\n394\t  DCHECK(IsOpen());\n395\t  DCHECK(cb);\n396\t\n397\t  uint32_t event_mask = POLLERR | POLLHUP;\n398\t\n399\t  Proactor* p = GetProactor();\n400\t  error_cb_wrapper_ = ErrorCbRefWrapper::New(std::move(cb));\n401\t  auto se_cb = [data = error_cb_wrapper_](detail::FiberInterface*, Proactor::IoResult res,\n402\t                                          uint32_t flags, uint32_t) {\n403\t    // Mark the poll id invalid before Destroy so a subsequent CancelOnErrorCb\n404\t    // on the socket skips PrepPollRemove on what may now be a reused\n405\t    // centries_ slot.\n406\t    data->error_cb_id = ErrorCbRefWrapper::kInvalidErrorCbId;\n407\t    auto cb = std::move(data->cb);\n408\t    ErrorCbRefWrapper::Destroy(data);\n409\t    if (res < 0) {\n410\t      res = -res;\n411\t      LOG_IF(WARNING, res != ECANCELED) << \"Unexpected error result \" << res;\n412\t    } else if (cb) {\n413\t      cb(res);\n414\t    }\n415\t  };\n416\t\n417\t  SubmitEntry se = p->GetSubmitEntry(std::move(se_cb));\n418\t  se.PrepPollAdd(ShiftedFd(), event_mask);\n419\t  se.sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n420\t\n421\t  // user_data is 32 bits because submit_tag is 0.\n422\t  error_cb_wrapper_->error_cb_id = se.sqe()->user_data;\n423\t}\n424\t\n425\tvoid UringSocket::CancelOnErrorCb() {\n426\t  if (!error_cb_wrapper_)\n427\t    return;\n428\t\n429\t  // If the error-poll completion already fired, the centries_ slot identified\n430\t  // by error_cb_id has been freed and may have been reused by a subsequent\n431\t  // submission on this proactor. Issuing PrepPollRemove on that user_data now\n432\t  // would cancel an unrelated request. Just release our reference.\n433\t  if (!error_cb_wrapper_->IsArmed()) {\n434\t    ErrorCbRefWrapper::Destroy(error_cb_wrapper_);\n435\t    error_cb_wrapper_ = nullptr;\n436\t    return;\n437\t calling.\n450\t    LOG_IF(WARNING, io_res != ENOENT && io_res != EALREADY)\n451\t        << \"Error canceling error cb \" << io_res;\n452\t  }\n453\t}\n454\t\n455\tvoid UringSocket::RegisterOnRecv(OnRecvCb cb) {\n456\t  DCHECK(IsOpen());\n457\t  CHECK_EQ(recv_poll_id_, 0u);\n458\t  CHECK(!register_recv_multishot_);\n459\t\n460\t  Proactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n461\t\n462\t  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n463\t  if (enable_multi_shot_) {\n464\t    auto recv_cb = [this, cb = std::move(cb)](detail::FiberInterface*, UringProactor::IoResult res,\n465\t                                              uint32_t flags, uint32_t bufring_id) {\n466\t      UringProactor* up = static_cast<UringProactor*>(ProactorBase::me());\n467\t\n468\t      if (flags & IORING_CQE_F_BUFFER) {\n469\t        CHECK_GT(res, 0);\n470\t\n471\t        uint16_t bufid = UringProactor::BufRingIdFromFlags(flags);\n472\t        bool incremental = (flags & IORING_CQE_F_BUF_MORE) != 0;\n473\t        DVLOG(2) << \"Received \" << bufid << \" \" << res << \" \" << incremental;\n474\t\n475\t        while (res > 0) {\n476\t          auto [buf_ptr, segment_len] = up->BufRingGetBufPtr(bufring_id, bufid, res);\n477\t          DVLOG(2) << \"Notifying \" << bufid << \" \" << res << \" \" << segment_len;\n478\t          cb(RecvNotification{io::MutableBytes(buf_ptr, segment_len)});\n479\t\n480\t          // Returns next bufid that comprises the bundle.\n481\t          bufid = up->BufRingTrackRecvCompletion(bufring_id, segment_len, bufid, incremental);\n482\t          res -= segment_len;\n483\t          incremental = true;  // after the first segment in a bundle we are always incremental.\n484\t        }\n485\t\n486\t        if ((flags & IORING_CQE_F_MORE) == 0) {\n487\t          // Kernel stopped multishot recv for some reason. Most likely due to lack of buffers.\n488\t          // We have two options here:\n489\t          // a) to reregister the recv using provided bufring_id.\n490\t          // b) to fallback to epoll registration.\n491\t          // For now we go with a).\n492\t          this->RegisterOnRecv(std::move(cb));  // re-register for next notifications.\n493\t        }\n494\t      } else {\n495\t        CHECK_LE(res, 0);\n496\t        res = -res;\n497\t        DCHECK_EQ(0u, flags & IORING_CQE_F_MORE);\n498\t\n499\t        if (res == ENOBUFS) {\n500\t          // TODO: we should reregister the recv here, there is no need to for the\n501\t          // app to know about it. There are two options:\n502\t          // a) to fallback to epoll or\n503\t          // b) to reissue recv with buffer select.\n504\t          LOG(FATAL) << \"TBD\";\n505\t        }\n506\t\n507\t        // When we get an error, the multishot stops automatically.\n508\t        // we align by resetting register_recv_multishot_ and propagate the error.\n509\t        this->register_recv_multishot_ = 0;\n510\t        if (res == 0) {\n511\t          // connection closed.\n512\t          cb(RecvNotification{false});\n513\t        } else {\n514\t          cb(RecvNotification{error_code(-res, system_category())});\n515\t        }\n516\t      }\n517\t    };\n518\t\n519\t    fb2::SubmitEntry entry = proactor->GetSubmitEntry(std::move(recv_cb), bufring_id_);\n520\t    entry.PrepRecv(fd, nullptr, 0, 0);\n521\t    auto& sqe = *entry.sqe();\n522\t\n523\t    sqe.flags |= (register_flag() | IOSQE_BUFFER_SELECT);\n524\t    sqe.buf_group = bufring_id_;\n525\t    uint16_t prio_flags = (IORING_RECV_MULTISHOT | IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST);\n526\t\n527\t    if (proactor->HasBundleSupport()) {\n528\t      prio_flags |= IORING_RECVSEND_BUNDLE;\n529\t    }\n530\t    sqe.ioprio |= prio_flags;\n531\t  } else {\n532\t    auto epoll_cb = [cb = std::move(cb)](uint32_t mask) {\n533\t      bool is_valid = (mask & (POLLERR | POLLHUP)) == 0;\n534\t      cb(RecvNotification{is_valid});\n535\t    };\n536\t\n537\t    recv_poll_id_ = proactor->EpollAdd(fd, std::move(epoll_cb), POLLIN);\n538\t  }\n539\t}\n540\t\n541\tvoid UringSocket::ResetOnRecvHook() {\n542\t  if (recv_poll_id_) {\n543\t    Proactor* p = GetProactor();\n544\t    p->EpollDel(recv_poll_id_);\n545\t    recv_poll_id_ = 0;\n546\t  } else if (register_recv_multishot_) {\n547\t    register_recv_multishot_ = 0;\n548\t    int flags = is_direct_fd_ ? IORING_ASYNC_CANCEL_FD_FIXED : IORING_ASYNC_CANCEL_FD;\n549\t\n550\t    int res = GetProactor()->CancelRequests(ShiftedFd(), flags);\n551\t    LOG_IF(WARNING, res < 0) << \"Error canceling multishot \" << -res;\n552\t  }\n553\t}\n554\t\n555\tauto UringSocket::native_handle() const -> native_handle_type {\n556\t  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n557\t\n558\t  if (is_direct_fd_) {\n559\t    fd = GetProactor()->TranslateDirectFd(fd);\n560\t  }\n561\t  return fd;\n562\t}\n563\t\n564\tvoid UringSocket::OnSetProactor() {\n565\t  UringProactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n566\t\n567\t  if (kEnableDirect && proactor->HasDirectFD() && is_direct_fd_ == 0 && fd_ >= 0) {\n568\t    // Using direct descriptors has consistent positive impact on CPU usage of the server.\n569\t    // Checked with echo_server with server side sockets.\n570\t    int source_fd = ShiftedFd();  // linux fd.\n571\t    unsigned direct_fd = proactor->RegisterFd(source_fd);\n572\t    if (direct_fd != UringProactor::kInvalidDirectFd) {\n573\t      // encode back the id we got.\n574\t      UpdateDfVal(direct_fd);\n575\t      is_direct_fd_ = 1;\n576\t    }\n577\t  }\n578\t}\n579\t\n580\tvoid UringSocket::OnResetProactor() {\n581\t  DCHECK(proactor()->InMyThread());\n582\t  if (is_direct_fd_) {\n583\t    UringProactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n584\t    unsigned direct_fd = ShiftedFd();\n585\t    int fd = proactor->UnregisterFd(direct_fd);\n586\t    if (fd < 0) {\n587\t      LOG(WARNING) << \"Error unregistering fd \" << direct_fd;\n588\t    }\n589\t    UpdateDfVal(fd);\n590\t    is_direct_fd_ = 0;\n591\t  }\n592\t}\n593\t\n594\t}  // namespace fb2\n595\t}  // namespace util\n596\t"}]},"uuid":"f7d05ecd-35f3-41ca-802e-498c7b72f722","timestamp":"2026-07-07T12:06:59.228Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/shared/Projects/helio-src/util/fibers/uring_socket.cc","content":"// Copyright 2023, Roman Gershman.  All rights reserved.\n// See LICENSE for licensing terms.\n//\n\n#include \"util/fibers/uring_socket.h\"\n\n#include <netinet/in.h>\n#include <poll.h>\n\n#include \"base/logging.h\"\n#include \"base/stl_util.h\"\n\n#define VSOCK(verbosity) VLOG(verbosity) << \"sock[\" << native_handle() << \"] \"\n#define DVSOCK(verbosity) DVLOG(verbosity) << \"sock[\" << native_handle() << \"] \"\n\nnamespace util {\nnamespace fb2 {\n\nusing namespace std;\nusing IoResult = UringProactor::IoResult;\nusing nonstd::make_unexpected;\n\nnamespace {\n\n// Disable direct fd for sockets due to https://github.com/axboe/liburing/issues/1192\nconstexpr bool kEnableDirect = false;\n\n}  // namespace\n\nUringSocket::UringSocket(int fd, Proactor* p) : LinuxSocketBase(fd, p), flags_(0) {\n  if (p) {\n    // This flag has a clear positive impact of the CPU usage for server side sockets.\n    // It also has a negative impact on client side sockets - not sure what the reason is but\n    // it was consistently reproducible with echo_server.\n    has_pollfirst_ = p->HasPollFirst();\n  }\n}\n\nUringSocket::~UringSocket() {\n  DCHECK_LT(fd_, 0) << \"Socket must have been closed explicitly.\";\n  error_code ec = Close();  // Quietly close.\n\n  LOG_IF(WARNING, ec) << \"Error closing socket \" << ec << \"/\" << ec.message();\n}\n\nerror_code UringSocket::Create(unsigned short protocol_family) {\n  error_code ec = LinuxSocketBase::Create(protocol_family);\n  if (ec) {\n    return ec;\n  }\n\n  UringProactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n  CHECK(proactor && is_direct_fd_ == 0);\n  DCHECK(proactor->InMyThread());\n\n  if (kEnableDirect && proactor->HasDirectFD()) {\n    int source_fd = ShiftedFd();  // linux fd.\n    unsigned direct_fd = proactor->RegisterFd(source_fd);\n    if (direct_fd != UringProactor::kInvalidDirectFd) {\n      // encode back the id we got.\n      UpdateDfVal(direct_fd);\n      is_direct_fd_ = 1;\n    }\n  }\n  return ec;\n}\n\nauto UringSocket::Close() -> error_code {\n  if (fd_ < 0)\n    return {};\n\n  // We may close a socket that was never registered with a proactor.\n  DCHECK(!proactor() || proactor()->InMyThread());\n  DVSOCK(1) << \"Closing socket\";\n\n  if (error_cb_wrapper_) {\n    LOG(DFATAL) << \"Error callback still registered in Close\";\n    ErrorCbRefWrapper::Destroy(error_cb_wrapper_);\n    error_cb_wrapper_ = nullptr;\n  }\n\n  ResetOnRecvHook();\n\n  int fd;\n  if (is_direct_fd_) {\n    unsigned direct_fd = ShiftedFd();\n    UringProactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n\n    fd = proactor->UnregisterFd(direct_fd);\n    if (fd < 0) {\n      LOG(WARNING) << \"Error unregistering fd \" << direct_fd;\n      return {};\n    }\n    is_direct_fd_ = 0;\n  } else {\n    fd = native_handle();\n  }\n\n  error_code ec;\n  posix_err_wrap(::close(fd), &ec);\n  fd_ = -1;\n\n  return ec;\n}\n\nauto UringSocket::Accept() -> AcceptResult {\n  CHECK(proactor());\n\n  error_code ec;\n\n  int fd = native_handle();\n  VSOCK(2) << \"Accept\";\n\n  int res = -1;\n  while (true) {\n    res = accept4(fd, NULL, NULL, SOCK_NONBLOCK | SOCK_CLOEXEC);\n    if (res >= 0) {\n      break;\n    }\n\n    DCHECK_EQ(-1, res);\n\n    if (errno == EAGAIN) {\n      // TODO: to add support for iouring direct file descriptors.\n      FiberCall fc(GetProactor());\n      fc->PrepPollAdd(ShiftedFd(), POLLIN);\n      fc->sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n      IoResult io_res = fc.Get();\n\n      // tcp sockets set POLLERR but UDS set POLLHUP.\n      if ((io_res & (POLLERR | POLLHUP)) != 0) {\n        return MakeUnexpected(errc::connection_aborted);\n      }\n\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    posix_err_wrap(res, &ec);\n    return make_unexpected(ec);\n  }\n\n  UringSocket* fs = new UringSocket{nullptr};\n  fs->fd_ = (res << kFdShift) | (fd_ & kInheritedFlags);\n  fs->has_pollfirst_ = has_pollfirst_;\n\n  return fs;\n}\n\nerror_code UringSocket::Connect(const endpoint_type& ep, std::function<void(int)> on_pre_connect) {\n  CHECK_EQ(fd_, -1);\n  CHECK(proactor() && proactor()->InMyThread());\n\n  error_code ec;\n\n  int fd = socket(ep.protocol().family(), SOCK_STREAM | SOCK_NONBLOCK | SOCK_CLOEXEC, IPPROTO_TCP);\n  if (posix_err_wrap(fd, &ec))\n    return ec;\n\n  VSOCK(1) << \"Connect [\" << fd << \"] \" << ep.address().to_string() << \":\" << ep.port();\n\n  UringProactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n\n  // TODO: support direct descriptors. For now client sockets always use regular linux fds.\n  fd_ = fd << kFdShift;\n\n  if (on_pre_connect) {\n    on_pre_connect(fd);\n  }\n\n  FiberCall fc(proactor, timeout());\n  fc->PrepConnect(fd, (const sockaddr*)ep.data(), ep.size());\n  IoResult io_res = fc.Get();\n\n  if (io_res < 0) {  // In that case connect returns -errno.\n    ec = error_code(-io_res, system_category());\n  }\n  return ec;\n}\n\nauto UringSocket::WriteSome(const iovec* ptr, uint32_t len) -> Result<size_t> {\n  DCHECK_GT(len, 0U);\n  DCHECK_GE(fd_, 0);\n\n  if (fd_ & IS_SHUTDOWN) {\n    return MakeUnexpected(errc::connection_aborted);\n  }\n\n  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n  Proactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n  DCHECK(ProactorBase::me() == proactor);\n\n  ssize_t res = 0;\n  VSOCK(2) << \"WriteSome [\" << fd << \"] \" << len;\n\n  if (len == 1) {\n    while (true) {\n      FiberCall fc(proactor, timeout());\n      fc->PrepSend(fd, ptr->iov_base, ptr->iov_len, MSG_NOSIGNAL);\n      fc->sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n      res = fc.Get();  // Interrupt point\n      if (res >= 0) {\n        return res;  // Fastpath\n      }\n\n      DVSOCK(2) << \"Got \" << res;\n      res = -res;\n      if (res == EAGAIN)  // EAGAIN can happen in case of CQ overflow.\n        continue;\n\n      break;\n    }\n  } else {  // len > 1\n    msghdr msg;\n    memset(&msg, 0, sizeof(msg));\n    msg.msg_iov = const_cast<iovec*>(ptr);\n    msg.msg_iovlen = len;\n\n    while (true) {\n      FiberCall fc(proactor, timeout());\n      fc->PrepSendMsg(fd, &msg, MSG_NOSIGNAL);\n      fc->sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n      res = fc.Get();  // Interrupt point\n      if (res >= 0) {\n        return res;  // Fastpath\n      }\n\n      DVSOCK(2) << \"Got \" << res;\n      res = -res;\n      if (res == EAGAIN)  // EAGAIN can happen in case of CQ overflow.\n        continue;\n\n      break;\n    };\n  }\n\n  error_code ec(res, system_category());\n  VSOCK(1) << \"Error \" << ec << \" on \" << RemoteEndpoint();\n\n  return make_unexpected(std::move(ec));\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::AsyncWriteSome(const iovec* v, uint32_t len, io::AsyncProgressCb cb) {\n  if (fd_ & IS_SHUTDOWN) {\n    cb(MakeUnexpected(errc::connection_aborted));\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // this time we can not store it on stack.\n  //\n  msghdr* msg = new msghdr;\n  memset(msg, 0, sizeof(msghdr));\n  msg->msg_iov = const_cast<iovec*>(v);\n  msg->msg_iovlen = len;\n\n  int fd = native_handle();\n  Proactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n  DCHECK(ProactorBase::me() == proactor);\n\n  auto mycb = [msg, cb = std::move(cb)](detail::FiberInterface*, Proactor::IoResult res,\n                                        uint32_t flags, uint32_t) {\n    delete msg;\n\n    if (res >= 0) {\n      cb(res);\n      return;\n    }\n\n    cb(make_unexpected(error_code{-res, generic_category()}));\n  };\n\n  SubmitEntry se = proactor->GetSubmitEntry(std::move(mycb));\n  se.PrepSendMsg(fd, msg, MSG_NOSIGNAL);\n  se.sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::AsyncReadSome(const iovec* v, uint32_t len, io::AsyncProgressCb cb) {\n  if (fd_ & IS_SHUTDOWN) {\n    cb(MakeUnexpected(errc::connection_aborted));\n    return;\n  }\n\n  msghdr* msg = new msghdr;\n  memset(msg, 0, sizeof(msghdr));\n  msg->msg_iov = const_cast<iovec*>(v);\n  msg->msg_iovlen = len;\n\n  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n  Proactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n  DCHECK(ProactorBase::me() == proactor);\n\n  auto mycb = [msg, cb = std::move(cb)](detail::FiberInterface*, Proactor::IoResult res,\n                                        uint32_t flags, uint32_t) {\n    delete msg;\n\n    if (res >= 0) {\n      cb(res);\n      return;\n    }\n\n    cb(make_unexpected(error_code{-res, generic_category()}));\n  };\n\n  SubmitEntry se = proactor->GetSubmitEntry(std::move(mycb));\n  se.PrepRecvMsg(fd, msg, 0);\n  se.sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n}\n\nauto UringSocket::RecvMsg(const msghdr& msg, int flags) -> Result<size_t> {\n  DCHECK_GE(fd_, 0);\n\n  if (fd_ & IS_SHUTDOWN) {\n    return MakeUnexpected(errc::connection_aborted);\n  }\n  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n  Proactor* p = GetProactor();\n  DCHECK(ProactorBase::me() == p);\n\n  ssize_t res;\n  VSOCK(2) << \"RecvMsg [\" << fd << \"]\";\n\n  while (true) {\n    FiberCall fc(p, timeout());\n    fc->PrepRecvMsg(fd, &msg, flags);\n    fc->sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n\n    // As described in \"io_uring_prep_recv(3)\"\n    if (has_pollfirst_ && !has_recv_data_) {\n      fc->sqe()->ioprio |= IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST;\n    }\n\n    res = fc.Get();\n\n    if (res >= 0) {\n      return res;\n    }\n    DVSOCK(2) << \"Got \" << res;\n\n    res = -res;\n    // EAGAIN can happen in case of CQ overflow.\n    if (res == EAGAIN && (flags & MSG_DONTWAIT) == 0) {\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    break;\n  }\n\n  error_code ec(res, system_category());\n  VSOCK(1) << \"Error \" << ec << \" on \" << RemoteEndpoint();\n\n  return make_unexpected(std::move(ec));\n}\n\nio::Result<size_t> UringSocket::Recv(const io::MutableBytes& mb, int flags) {\n  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n  Proactor* p = GetProactor();\n  DCHECK(ProactorBase::me() == p);\n\n  VSOCK(2) << \"Recv [\" << fd << \"], flags: \" << flags;\n  ssize_t res;\n  while (true) {\n    FiberCall fc(p, timeout());\n    fc->PrepRecv(fd, mb.data(), mb.size(), flags);\n    fc->sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n    if (has_pollfirst_ && !has_recv_data_) {\n      fc->sqe()->ioprio |= IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST;\n    }\n    res = fc.Get();\n\n    if (res >= 0) {\n      has_recv_data_ = (fc.flags() & IORING_CQE_F_SOCK_NONEMPTY) ? 1 : 0;\n      DVSOCK(2) << \"Received \" << res << \" bytes \"\n                << \", has_more \" << has_recv_data_;\n      return res;\n    }\n    DVSOCK(2) << \"Got \" << res;\n\n    res = -res;\n    // EAGAIN can happen in case of CQ overflow.\n    if (res == EAGAIN && (flags & MSG_DONTWAIT) == 0) {\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    break;\n  }\n\n  error_code ec(res, system_category());\n  VSOCK(1) << \"Error \" << ec << \" on \" << RemoteEndpoint();\n\n  return make_unexpected(std::move(ec));\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::RegisterOnErrorCb(std::function<void(uint32_t)> cb) {\n  CHECK(!error_cb_wrapper_);\n  DCHECK(IsOpen());\n  DCHECK(cb);\n\n  uint32_t event_mask = POLLERR | POLLHUP;\n\n  Proactor* p = GetProactor();\n  error_cb_wrapper_ = ErrorCbRefWrapper::New(std::move(cb));\n  auto se_cb = [data = error_cb_wrapper_](detail::FiberInterface*, Proactor::IoResult res,\n                                          uint32_t flags, uint32_t) {\n    // Mark the poll id invalid before Destroy so a subsequent CancelOnErrorCb\n    // on the socket skips PrepPollRemove on what may now be a reused\n    // centries_ slot.\n    data->error_cb_id = ErrorCbRefWrapper::kInvalidErrorCbId;\n    auto cb = std::move(data->cb);\n    ErrorCbRefWrapper::Destroy(data);\n    if (res < 0) {\n      res = -res;\n      LOG_IF(WARNING, res != ECANCELED) << \"Unexpected error result \" << res;\n    } else if (cb) {\n      cb(res);\n    }\n  };\n\n  SubmitEntry se = p->GetSubmitEntry(std::move(se_cb));\n  se.PrepPollAdd(ShiftedFd(), event_mask);\n  se.sqe()->flags |= register_flag();\n\n  // user_data is 32 bits because submit_tag is 0.\n  error_cb_wrapper_->error_cb_id = se.sqe()->user_data;\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::CancelOnErrorCb() {\n  if (!error_cb_wrapper_)\n    return;\n\n  // If the error-poll completion already fired, the centries_ slot identified\n  // by error_cb_id has been freed and may have been reused by a subsequent\n  // submission on this proactor. Issuing PrepPollRemove on that user_data now\n  // would cancel an unrelated request. Just release our reference.\n  if (!error_cb_wrapper_->IsArmed()) {\n    ErrorCbRefWrapper::Destroy(error_cb_wrapper_);\n    error_cb_wrapper_ = nullptr;\n    return;\n  }\n\n  FiberCall fc(GetProactor());\n  fc->PrepPollRemove(error_cb_wrapper_->error_cb_id);\n\n  ErrorCbRefWrapper::Destroy(error_cb_wrapper_);\n  error_cb_wrapper_ = nullptr;\n\n  IoResult io_res = fc.Get();\n  if (io_res < 0) {\n    io_res = -io_res;\n\n    // The callback could have been already called or being in process of calling.\n    LOG_IF(WARNING, io_res != ENOENT && io_res != EALREADY)\n        << \"Error canceling error cb \" << io_res;\n  }\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::RegisterOnRecv(OnRecvCb cb) {\n  DCHECK(IsOpen());\n  CHECK_EQ(recv_poll_id_, 0u);\n  CHECK(!register_recv_multishot_);\n\n  Proactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n\n  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n  if (enable_multi_shot_) {\n    auto recv_cb = [this, cb = std::move(cb)](detail::FiberInterface*, UringProactor::IoResult res,\n                                              uint32_t flags, uint32_t bufring_id) {\n      UringProactor* up = static_cast<UringProactor*>(ProactorBase::me());\n\n      if (flags & IORING_CQE_F_BUFFER) {\n        CHECK_GT(res, 0);\n\n        uint16_t bufid = UringProactor::BufRingIdFromFlags(flags);\n        bool incremental = (flags & IORING_CQE_F_BUF_MORE) != 0;\n        DVLOG(2) << \"Received \" << bufid << \" \" << res << \" \" << incremental;\n\n        while (res > 0) {\n          auto [buf_ptr, segment_len] = up->BufRingGetBufPtr(bufring_id, bufid, res);\n          DVLOG(2) << \"Notifying \" << bufid << \" \" << res << \" \" << segment_len;\n          cb(RecvNotification{io::MutableBytes(buf_ptr, segment_len)});\n\n          // Returns next bufid that comprises the bundle.\n          bufid = up->BufRingTrackRecvCompletion(bufring_id, segment_len, bufid, incremental);\n          res -= segment_len;\n          incremental = true;  // after the first segment in a bundle we are always incremental.\n        }\n\n        if ((flags & IORING_CQE_F_MORE) == 0) {\n          // Kernel stopped multishot recv for some reason. Most likely due to lack of buffers.\n          // We have two options here:\n          // a) to reregister the recv using provided bufring_id.\n          // b) to fallback to epoll registration.\n          // For now we go with a).\n          this->RegisterOnRecv(std::move(cb));  // re-register for next notifications.\n        }\n      } else {\n        CHECK_LE(res, 0);\n        res = -res;\n        DCHECK_EQ(0u, flags & IORING_CQE_F_MORE);\n\n        if (res == ENOBUFS) {\n          // TODO: we should reregister the recv here, there is no need to for the\n          // app to know about it. There are two options:\n          // a) to fallback to epoll or\n          // b) to reissue recv with buffer select.\n          LOG(FATAL) << \"TBD\";\n        }\n\n        // When we get an error, the multishot stops automatically.\n        // we align by resetting register_recv_multishot_ and propagate the error.\n        this->register_recv_multishot_ = 0;\n        if (res == 0) {\n          // connection closed.\n          cb(RecvNotification{false});\n        } else {\n          cb(RecvNotification{error_code(-res, system_category())});\n        }\n      }\n    };\n\n    fb2::SubmitEntry entry = proactor->GetSubmitEntry(std::move(recv_cb), bufring_id_);\n    entry.PrepRecv(fd, nullptr, 0, 0);\n    auto& sqe = *entry.sqe();\n\n    sqe.flags |= (register_flag() | IOSQE_BUFFER_SELECT);\n    sqe.buf_group = bufring_id_;\n    uint16_t prio_flags = (IORING_RECV_MULTISHOT | IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST);\n\n    if (proactor->HasBundleSupport()) {\n      prio_flags |= IORING_RECVSEND_BUNDLE;\n    }\n    sqe.ioprio |= prio_flags;\n  } else {\n    auto epoll_cb = [cb = std::move(cb)](uint32_t mask) {\n      bool is_valid = (mask & (POLLERR | POLLHUP)) == 0;\n      cb(RecvNotification{is_valid});\n    };\n\n    recv_poll_id_ = proactor->EpollAdd(fd, std::move(epoll_cb), POLLIN);\n  }\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::ResetOnRecvHook() {\n  if (recv_poll_id_) {\n    Proactor* p = GetProactor();\n    p->EpollDel(recv_poll_id_);\n    recv_poll_id_ = 0;\n  } else if (register_recv_multishot_) {\n    register_recv_multishot_ = 0;\n    int flags = is_direct_fd_ ? IORING_ASYNC_CANCEL_FD_FIXED : IORING_ASYNC_CANCEL_FD;\n\n    int res = GetProactor()->CancelRequests(ShiftedFd(), flags);\n    LOG_IF(WARNING, res < 0) << \"Error canceling multishot \" << -res;\n  }\n}\n\nauto UringSocket::native_handle() const -> native_handle_type {\n  int fd = ShiftedFd();\n\n  if (is_direct_fd_) {\n    fd = GetProactor()->TranslateDirectFd(fd);\n  }\n  return fd;\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::OnSetProactor() {\n  UringProactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n\n  if (kEnableDirect && proactor->HasDirectFD() && is_direct_fd_ == 0 && fd_ >= 0) {\n    // Using direct descriptors has consistent positive impact on CPU usage of the server.\n    // Checked with echo_server with server side sockets.\n    int source_fd = ShiftedFd();  // linux fd.\n    unsigned direct_fd = proactor->RegisterFd(source_fd);\n    if (direct_fd != UringProactor::kInvalidDirectFd) {\n      // encode back the id we got.\n      UpdateDfVal(direct_fd);\n      is_direct_fd_ = 1;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nvoid UringSocket::OnResetProactor() {\n  DCHECK(proactor()->InMyThread());\n  if (is_direct_fd_) {\n    UringProactor* proactor = GetProactor();\n    unsigned direct_fd = ShiftedFd();\n    int fd = proactor->UnregisterFd(direct_fd);\n    if (fd < 0) {\n      LOG(WARNING) << \"Error unregistering fd \" << direct_fd;\n    }\n    UpdateDfVal(fd);\n    is_direct_fd_ = 0;\n  }\n}\n\n}  // namespace fb2\n}  // namespace util\n","numLines":596,"startLine":1,"totalLines":596}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3c74573d-360b-4a09-a625-2db4931e789c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}

The kernel will silently clean up these empty connections without\n      // consuming Dragonfly fibers or OpenSSL memory.\n      // This imposes zero latency penalty on well-behaved clients, as the kernel instantly\n      // yields the connection to user-space the moment their first byte (e.g., TLS ClientHello\n      // or RESP command) arrives.\n      static constexpr int kDeferAcceptTimeoutSec = 1;\n      if (setsockopt(fd, IPPROTO_TCP, TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT, &kDeferAcceptTimeoutSec,\n                     sizeof(kDeferAcceptTimeoutSec)) < 0) {\n        LOG(WARNING) << \"Could not set TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT \" << SafeErrorMessage(errno);\n      }\n    }\n  }\n#endif\n  bool success = ConfigureKeepAlive(fd);\n\n#ifdef __linux__\n  int user_timeout = absl::GetFlag(FLAGS_tcp_user_timeout);\n  if (setsockopt(fd, IPPROTO_TCP, TCP_USER_TIMEOUT, &user_timeout, sizeof(int)) < 0) {\n    LOG(WARNING) << \"Could not set user timeout on socket \" << SafeErrorMessage(errno);\n  }\n#endif\n\n  if (!success) {\n#ifndef __APPLE__\n    int myerr = errno;\n\n    int socket_type;\n    socklen_t length = sizeof(socket_type);\n\n    // Ignore the error on UDS.\n    if (getsockopt(fd, SOL_SOCKET, SO_DOMAIN, &socket_type, &length) != 0 ||\n        socket_type != AF_UNIX) {\n      LOG(WARNING) << \"Could not configure keep alive \" << SafeErrorMessage(myerr);\n    }\n#endif\n  }\n","numLines":60,"startLine":180,"totalLines":428}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0270d332-444d-4358-a66f-453cde53e2cb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}

fd = sock->native_handle();\n\n    int cpu, napi_id;\n    socklen_t len = sizeof(cpu);\n\n    // I suspect that the advantage of using SO_INCOMING_NAPI_ID is that\n    // we can also track the affinity changes during the lifetime of the process\n    // i.e. when a different CPU is assigned to handle the RX traffic.\n    // On some distributions (WSL1, for example), SO_INCOMING_CPU is not supported.\n    if (0 == getsockopt(fd, SOL_SOCKET, SO_INCOMING_CPU, &cpu, &len)) {\n      VLOG(1) << \"CPU for connection \" << fd << \" is \" << cpu;\n      // Avoid CHECKINGing success, it sometimes fail on WSL\n      // https://github.com/dragonflydb/dragonfly/issues/2090\n      if (0 == getsockopt(fd, SOL_SOCKET, SO_INCOMING_NAPI_ID, &napi_id, &len)) {\n        VLOG(1) << \"NAPI for connection \" << fd << \" is \" << napi_id;\n      }\n\n      if (GetFlag(FLAGS_conn_use_incoming_cpu)) {\n        // We choose a thread that is running on the incoming CPU. Usually there is\n        // a single thread per CPU. SO_INCOMING_CPU returns the CPU that the kernel\n        // uses to steer the packets to. In order to make\n        // conn_use_incoming_cpu effective, we should make sure that the receive packets are\n        // steered to enough CPUs. Must be enabled only in special cases.\n137\t      peer->SetProactor(sock_->proactor());\n138\t      std::ignore = peer->Close();\n139\t      continue;\n140\t    }\n141\t\n142\t    VSOCK(2, *peer) << \"Accepted \" << peer->RemoteEndpoint();\n143\t\n144\t    uint32_t prev_connections = open_connections_.fetch_add(1, memory_order_acquire);\n145\t    if (prev_connections >= max_clients_) {\n146\t      // In general, we do not handle max client limit correctly for case where we experience\n147\t      // connection storms. The reason is that we immediately close the just-accepted socket,\n148\t      // which causes clients just to retry again and again, and increases the load on the server.\n149\t      // What we need to do is to start throttling the accept queue by NOT handling\n150\t      // the accept requests. Then the accept queue overflows and the linux OS\n151\t      // starts blocking connection requests by dropping SYN packets, effectively stalling\n152\t      // the connecting clients.\n153\t      // Here is the great article explaining the dynamics of the connection storms:\n154\t      // https://veithen.io/2014/01/01/how-tcp-backlog-works-in-linux.html\n155\t      // There is this one as well: https://blog.cloudflare.com/when-tcp-sockets-refuse-to-die/\n156\t      peer->SetProactor(sock_->proactor());\n157\t      OnMaxConnectionsReached(peer.get());\n158\t      (void)peer->Close();\n159\t      open_connections_.fetch_sub(1, memory_order_release);\n160\t      continue;\n161\t    }\n162\t\n163\t    // Most probably next is in another thread.\n164\t    fb2::ProactorBase* next = PickConnectionProactor(peer.get());\n165\t"}]},"uuid":"08bd308f-d85e-4375-ac26-978e1d56073d","timestamp":"2026-07-07T12:07:59.100Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/shared/Projects/helio-src/util/fibers/listener_interface.cc","content":"void ListenerInterface::RunAcceptLoop() {\n  ThisFiber::SetName(\"AcceptLoop\");\n  FiberSocketBase::endpoint_type ep;\n\n  if (!sock_->IsUDS()) {\n    ep = sock_->LocalEndpoint();\n    VSOCK(0, *sock_) << \"AcceptServer - listening on \" << ep.address() << \":\" << ep.port();\n  }\n\n  PreAcceptLoop(sock_->proactor());\n\n  pool_->AwaitBrief([this](unsigned index, auto*) {\n    DVLOG(1) << \"Emplacing listener \" << this;\n    auto res = listener_map.emplace(this, new TLConnList{});\n    CHECK(res.second);\n    res.first->second->pool_index = index;\n  });\n\n  while (true) {\n    // For burst of accept requests we yield to allow other fibers to run.\n    if (base::CycleClock::ToUsec(ThisFiber::GetRunningTimeCycles()) > 1000) {  // 1ms\n      ThisFiber::Yield();\n    }\n\n    FiberSocketBase::AcceptResult res = sock_->Accept();\n    if (!res.has_value()) {\n      FiberSocketBase::error_code ec = res.error();\n      if (ec != errc::connection_aborted) {\n        LOG(WARNING) << \"Error calling accept \" << ec << \"/\" << ec.message();\n      }\n      VLOG(1) << \"Breaking RunAcceptLoop()\";\n      break;\n    }\n\n    unique_ptr<FiberSocketBase> peer{res.value()};\n\n    if (pause_accepting_) {\n      // Immediately closes the incoming connection.\n      // Please note that this mode could trigger a dangerous dynamic like a connection storm,\n      // where many clients try to reconnect again and again, causing a pressure on the host's\n      // firewall. Must be enabled only in special cases.\n      peer->SetProactor(sock_->proactor());\n      std::ignore = peer->Close();\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    VSOCK(2, *peer) << \"Accepted \" << peer->RemoteEndpoint();\n\n    uint32_t prev_connections = open_connections_.fetch_add(1, memory_order_acquire);\n    if (prev_connections >= max_clients_) {\n      // In general, we do not handle max client limit correctly for case where we experience\n      // connection storms. The reason is that we immediately close the just-accepted socket,\n      // which causes clients just to retry again and again, and increases the load on the server.\n      // What we need to do is to start throttling the accept queue by NOT handling\n      // the accept requests. Then the accept queue overflows and the linux OS\n      // starts blocking connection requests by dropping SYN packets, effectively stalling\n      // the connecting clients.\n      // Here is the great article explaining the dynamics of the connection storms:\n      // https://veithen.io/2014/01/01/how-tcp-backlog-works-in-linux.html\n      // There is this one as well: https://blog.cloudflare.com/when-tcp-sockets-refuse-to-die/\n      peer->SetProactor(sock_->proactor());\n      OnMaxConnectionsReached(peer.get());\n      (void)peer->Close();\n      open_connections_.fetch_sub(1, memory_order_release);\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    // Most probably next is in another thread.\n    fb2::ProactorBase* next = PickConnectionProactor(peer.get());\n","numLines":70,"startLine":96,"totalLines":464}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"29605848-f1b5-4388-bff3-4b7e2bf88df0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}

{"parentUuid":"d994f75c-bce4-42a7-bb94-040e45e4d9cf","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"9b351eeb-5b06-4fc5-a6b7-58da839e4ef5","agentId":"adeb237427453514e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0166ErTJgqrSQK35e2w91XhD","type":"tool_result","content":"=== branch ===\n2s-deep-uring-dev\n=== log for the two commits ===\n4315accefff9a94c76a7bfec35cabf970a95c364\nhenry\nMon Jul 6 07:21:44 2026 -0700\ndeep-uring (U2, multishot-recv hardening): runtime-sized provided-buffer ring + POLL_FIRST + verified ENOBUFS re-arm.\n\n(a) tomokv-uring-bufring (new int knob, IMMUTABLE, default 0=auto): sizes the per-IO-thread provided-buffer ring that backs multishot recv. 0 -> the old hardwired 512; N is rounded UP to a power of two (buf_ring requirement) and clamped [64, 65536]; the boot line reports the effective count and whether it was rounded. CQ sized to max(IOU_RECV_DEPTH, 2*nbufs) via IORING_SETUP_CQSIZE so a full-burst of per-buffer CQEs cannot overflow. (b) IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST on every multishot arm/re-arm (iouRecvPrepArm): request/response traffic almost never has bytes queued at arm time, so skip the speculative in-line recv attempt and go straight to the poll wait (VLDB'26 io_uring guidance for RPC shapes); data already queued just completes the poll immediately - no semantic change. (c) ENOBUFS re-arm audit (task-required verification): the path EXISTS and is safe by construction - buffers are only held between CQE post and the same reap pass's iouRecvProvide, so by the end-of-pass re-arm submit the ring has space; added a log-once that names the knob when exhaustion is hit (sizing signal for the real-NIC eval). (d) Recv ring setup deliberately stays PLAIN (documented in-code + boot line): iouRecvDisarm must submit the multishot ASYNC_CANCEL from the MAIN thread inside the pauseIOThread window on client migration (the W6-U2 input-loss fix), and SINGLE_ISSUER|DEFER_TASKRUN rings reject every cross-task ring op - probed on this 6.17 kernel: non-owner submit AND non-owner IORING_REGISTER_SYNC_CANCEL both return -EINVAL - while deferring the cancel to the owner would reopen the exact byte-loss window W6-U2 closed. Smoke (6403, contended box): io4ex4 + tomokv-io-uring/-recv, bufring 256 honored per log, SET/GET/MGET + 3000B/15000B byte-exact + 2416 fresh-conn pipe loops, 0 fails, 0 asserts.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nClaude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01MAsgpd12Zk4h6ceN8dHdEi\n\n\n src/config.c     |  3 +++\n src/networking.c | 75 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++------------\n src/server.h     |  1 +\n 3 files changed, 64 insertions(+), 15 deletions(-)\n======\n5c77d7b32ffad9d2391cc1e3d4800662085595dc\ndeep-uring (U1+U3, one coherent send-path redesign): ring hygiene (SINGLE_ISSUER|DEFER_TASKRUN + registered ring fd + one submit_and_wait per pass) + zero-copy send rebuilt as detach-on-submit with ASYNC notif reaping and a registered buffer pool.\n\nU1 RING HYGIENE (send rings; per-io-thread + main): setup now probes IORING_SETUP_SINGLE_ISSUER|IORING_SETUP_DEFER_TASKRUN and falls back to a plain ring if the kernel refuses, with a boot line naming which mode each thread got (SQPOLL knob keeps its legacy exclusive branch - deferred task work needs the submitter to enter the kernel, SQPOLL's point is not entering). io_uring_register_ring_fd() after every successful setup (skips per-enter fdget/fdput; valid because these rings are single-threaded by design - all submits/reaps/registrations happen on the owning thread). Batched-submit audit: the reply-send path already collected SQEs across the whole beforeSleep pass with ONE submit (v11-B design, v12-J routes worker replies into it); that submit is now io_uring_submit_and_wait(ring,1) - submit + first-completion wait in one syscall, and the wait IS genuinely needed here because the pass synchronously consumes all send RESULTS (plain-send buffer reuse depends on it). Remaining per-SQE submits are cold by nature and kept immediate for correctness: recv arm (once per new connection), reaper re-arm (once per reap pass), disarm cancel (rare, and racing it to a batch would reopen W6-U2). Hard/partial submit failures now abort the pass safely instead of risking a wait_cqe block or carrying stale user_data SQEs into a later pass (dropped remainder logged; W6-U3 abort posture).\n\nU3 SEND PATH / ZC redesign: the old v12-H SEND_ZC waited synchronously for every IORING_CQE_F_NOTIF inside the flush pass (the sync-notif-wait design flaw: the pass serialized on NIC buffer release). Fix = DETACH-ON-SUBMIT: at ZC submit the client's c->buf moves into a per-thread inflight-entry table (entry owns buffer + registered-slot index; user_data = bit63-tagged entry idx, so heap client pointers can never collide) and the client immediately gets a fresh buffer with bufpos/sentlen reset - this IS the inflight-pin, expressed as ownership transfer: the pinned bytes are unreachable from every client-side path (writeToClient, addReply, resize cron, freeClient) by construction, no per-op pin checks needed anywhere. F_NOTIF CQEs are reaped asynchronously at the START of later passes (plus opportunistically mid-wait); notifs touch only entry-owned state, so a client freed between passes cannot UAF - the entry->client pointer is dereferenced exclusively for same-pass RESULTS, when liveness is guaranteed (freeClientsInAsyncFreeQueue runs after the pass). Partial/EAGAIN results copy the unsent tail from the entry buffer into the client's (empty) fresh buffer and hand off to epoll; the DMA range lives in the entry buffer so the copy cannot race it. REGISTERED BUFFERS (io_uring_register_buffers_sparse + per-slot update_tag, 128x16K lazily-allocated slots/thread): the detach-swap hands clients pool buffers, so steady-state ZC sends go out as SEND_ZC_FIXED (buf_index, no per-send page pin); pool exhausted -> plain heap swap + unregistered SEND_ZC (still zero-copy). Plain (non-ZC) sends cannot use buf_index (kernel limitation) and are unchanged. Slot lifecycle is owner-confined: freeClient returns slots directly (buf teardown routed through iouZcFreeClientBuf), client migration off an IO thread de-slots onto heap under the pauseIOThread window and queues the slot for the owner's next pass (iouZcOnClientUnbind in unbind+fetch), and clientsCronResizeOutputBuffer skips pool-backed buffers (registration is bound to that exact memory). Threshold knob tomokv-uring-zc-min (new, MODIFIABLE, default 1024 per the ZC->1KiB guidance) replaces the hardwired 4096; below it plain send's copy beats ZC's notif+bookkeeping. CLOSE_AFTER_REPLY is now honored on fully-sent uring results (plain + ZC) like writeToClient does. Fake-reply path note: fakes/encoded (BULK_STR_REF) replies never reach the ring - the fast-path gate requires !buf_encoded + empty reply list, and fake replies are copied into the real parent by AddReplyFromClient/csReassemble - so the bytes the kernel sees are always real-client-owned; there is nothing separate to pin on that path (it stays on the writev fallback, the S8-managed lineage).\n\nKernel probe on this 6.17 box (drove the design): SI|DTR setup OK; eventfd DOES fire under DEFER_TASKRUN when local task work is queued (reaper flushes visibility with io_uring_get_events, called only when ZC completions are outstanding); cross-task submit and cross-task SYNC_CANCEL on an SI|DTR ring both -EINVAL (why the recv ring stays plain, see U2 commit); sparse regbuf + update_tag + SEND_ZC_FIXED all work (SEND_ZC on AF_UNIX is EOPNOTSUPP - probe artifact, TCP is fine).\n\nValidation (6403, io4ex4, contended box): knob combos booted + SET/GET/MGET + 3000B/15000B byte-exact + ~20s fresh-conn pipe loops each - knobs-off epoll (4544 loops), io-uring alone (6745), +recv (6001), +reply-send (6401), +zc (6356), ALL knobs + bufring 100->128 + zc-min 512 (6436); zero fails, zero asserts everywhere. ZC verified actually firing (needs reply-send routing or inline big replies): first-send log + FIXED registered-slot sends on all 4 threads. ASAN build (SANITIZER=address USE_URING=yes), ALL uring knobs, 60s of 3 concurrent mixed loops = 8423 fresh-connection pipes (3K/8K ZC-sized values, expire family, dels) + 2324 CLIENT KILL mass-disconnect sweeps under ZC load: ZERO AddressSanitizer reports, counters exact (actr==loopA, bctr==loopB), server alive. Default-off audit: non-USE_URING build compiles+links with zero iou* symbols (all additions gated); USE_URING-knobs-off residuals are one predictable compare per connection-lifecycle event (create/free/migrate/cron-visit), zero changes on per-op hot paths.\n\nKnown residuals (documented, not new): the hybrid-handoff freeClient-from-main paths W6 already flagged remain out-of-scope (the defensive branch leaks a pool slot with a WARNING rather than racing the owner); a pass abort on hard wait_cqe error still wedges that pass's clients (pre-existing W6-U3 posture, now narrower).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nClaude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01MAsgpd12Zk4h6ceN8dHdEi\n\n\n src/config.c     |   8 +-\n src/iothread.c   |   7 +\n src/networking.c | 428 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++--------\n src/server.c     |  10 ++\n src/server.h     |   8 ++\n 5 files changed, 403 insertions(+), 58 deletions(-)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"df1a0088-991c-450d-9c0a-cde0f164b303","timestamp":"2026-07-07T12:08:14.578Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"=== branch ===\n2s-deep-uring-dev\n=== log for the two commits ===\n4315accefff9a94c76a7bfec35cabf970a95c364\nhenry\nMon Jul 6 07:21:44 2026 -0700\ndeep-uring (U2, multishot-recv hardening): runtime-sized provided-buffer ring + POLL_FIRST + verified ENOBUFS re-arm.\n\n(a) tomokv-uring-bufring (new int knob, IMMUTABLE, default 0=auto): sizes the per-IO-thread provided-buffer ring that backs multishot recv. 0 -> the old hardwired 512; N is rounded UP to a power of two (buf_ring requirement) and clamped [64, 65536]; the boot line reports the effective count and whether it was rounded. CQ sized to max(IOU_RECV_DEPTH, 2*nbufs) via IORING_SETUP_CQSIZE so a full-burst of per-buffer CQEs cannot overflow. (b) IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST on every multishot arm/re-arm (iouRecvPrepArm): request/response traffic almost never has bytes queued at arm time, so skip the speculative in-line recv attempt and go straight to the poll wait (VLDB'26 io_uring guidance for RPC shapes); data already queued just completes the poll immediately - no semantic change. (c) ENOBUFS re-arm audit (task-required verification): the path EXISTS and is safe by construction - buffers are only held between CQE post and the same reap pass's iouRecvProvide, so by the end-of-pass re-arm submit the ring has space; added a log-once that names the knob when exhaustion is hit (sizing signal for the real-NIC eval). (d) Recv ring setup deliberately stays PLAIN (documented in-code + boot line): iouRecvDisarm must submit the multishot ASYNC_CANCEL from the MAIN thread inside the pauseIOThread window on client migration (the W6-U2 input-loss fix), and SINGLE_ISSUER|DEFER_TASKRUN rings reject every cross-task ring op - probed on this 6.17 kernel: non-owner submit AND non-owner IORING_REGISTER_SYNC_CANCEL both return -EINVAL - while deferring the cancel to the owner would reopen the exact byte-loss window W6-U2 closed. Smoke (6403, contended box): io4ex4 + tomokv-io-uring/-recv, bufring 256 honored per log, SET/GET/MGET + 3000B/15000B byte-exact + 2416 fresh-conn pipe loops, 0 fails, 0 asserts.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nClaude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01MAsgpd12Zk4h6ceN8dHdEi\n\n\n src/config.c     |  3 +++\n src/networking.c | 75 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++------------\n src/server.h     |  1 +\n 3 files changed, 64 insertions(+), 15 deletions(-)\n======\n5c77d7b32ffad9d2391cc1e3d4800662085595dc\ndeep-uring (U1+U3, one coherent send-path redesign): ring hygiene (SINGLE_ISSUER|DEFER_TASKRUN + registered ring fd + one submit_and_wait per pass) + zero-copy send rebuilt as detach-on-submit with ASYNC notif reaping and a registered buffer pool.\n\nU1 RING HYGIENE (send rings; per-io-thread + main): setup now probes IORING_SETUP_SINGLE_ISSUER|IORING_SETUP_DEFER_TASKRUN and falls back to a plain ring if the kernel refuses, with a boot line naming which mode each thread got (SQPOLL knob keeps its legacy exclusive branch - deferred task work needs the submitter to enter the kernel, SQPOLL's point is not entering). io_uring_register_ring_fd() after every successful setup (skips per-enter fdget/fdput; valid because these rings are single-threaded by design - all submits/reaps/registrations happen on the owning thread). Ts> void SinkReplyBuilder::WritePieces(Ts&&... pieces) {\n  if (size_t required = (piece_size(pieces) + ...); buffer_.AppendLen() <= required)\n    Flush(required);\n\n  auto iovec_end = [](const iovec& v) { return reinterpret_cast<char*>(v.iov_base) + v.iov_len; };\n\n  // Ensure last iovec points to buffer segment\n  char* dest = reinterpret_cast<char*>(buffer_.AppendBuffer().data());\n  if (vecs_.empty()) {\n    vecs_.push_back(iovec{dest, 0});\n  } else if (iovec_end(vecs_.back()) != dest) {\n    if (vecs_.size() >= IOV_MAX - 2)\n      Flush();\n    dest = reinterpret_cast<char*>(buffer_.AppendBuffer().data());\n    vecs_.push_back(iovec{dest, 0});\n  }\n\n  DCHECK(iovec_end(vecs_.back()) == dest);\n  char* ptr = dest;\n  ([&]() { ptr = write_piece(pieces, ptr); }(), ...);\n\n  size_t written = ptr - dest;\n  buffer_.CommitWrite(written);\n  vecs_.back().iov_len += written;\n  total_size_ += written;\n}\n\nvoid SinkReplyBuilder::WriteDecodedAscii(const cmn::BorrowedString& bs) {\n  auto* ops = cmn::BorrowedStringOps::Get();\n  const size_t src_total = bs.view().size();\n  size_t src_offset = 0;\n\n  // Huge strings benefit from max scratch capacity. Going via Flush() (rather\n  // than EnsureCapacity directly) drains any pending iovecs first — otherwise\n  // a reallocate-grow would dangling-pointer the prefix iovec written by the\n  // caller into our own scratch.\n  if (buffer_.Capacity() < kMaxBufferSize)\n    Flush(kMaxBufferSize);\n\n  while (src_offset < src_total) {\n    // Ensure buffer_ has some room.\n    if (buffer_.AppendLen() < 64)\n      Flush();\n    if (ec_)\n      break;\n\n    char* dest = reinterpret_cast<char*>(buffer_.AppendBuffer().data());\n    auto r =\n        ops->DecodeChunk(bs, src_total, src_offset, std::span<char>(dest, buffer_.AppendLen()));\n    DCHECK_GT(r.dec_written, 0u);\n    WriteRef(std::string_view(dest, r.dec_written));\n\n    // dest lives inside our scratch — advance the buffer's write cursor so\n    // the next iteration's AppendBuffer().data() doesn't return the same\n    // address and overwrite the chunk we just queued.\n    buffer_.CommitWrite(r.dec_written);\n    src_offset += r.src_consumed;\n  }\n}\n\nvoid SinkReplyBuilder::WriteRef(std::string_view str) {\n  if (vecs_.size() >= IOV_MAX - 2)\n    Flush();\n  vecs_.push_back(iovec{const_cast<char*>(str.data()), str.size()});\n  total_size_ += str.size();\n}\n\nvoid SinkReplyBuilder::Flush(size_t expected_buffer_cap) {\n  // Fast path: nothing buffered and no buffer resize requested.\n  if (vecs_.empty() && (expected_buffer_cap == 0))\n    return;\n\n  if (!vecs_.empty())\n    Send();\n\n  // Grow backing buffer if was at least half full and still below it's max size\n  if (buffer_.InputLen() * 2 > buffer_.Capacity() && buffer_.Capacity() * 2 <= kMaxBufferSize)\n    expected_buffer_cap = max(expected_buffer_cap, buffer_.Capacity() * 2);\n\n  total_size_ = 0;\n  buffer_.Clear();\n  vecs_.clear();\n  guaranteed_pieces_ = 0;\n\n  DCHECK_LE(expected_buffer_cap, kMaxBufferSize);  // big strings should be enqueued as iovecs\n\n  if (expected_buffer_cap > buffer_.Capacity())\n    buffer_.Reserve(expected_buffer_cap);\n}\n\nuint64_t SinkReplyBuilder::GetLastSendTimeCycles() const {\n  return send_time_cycles_;\n}\n\nvoid SinkReplyBuilder::Send() {\n  DCHECK(sink_ != nullptr);\n  DCHECK(!vecs_.empty());\n  auto& reply_stats = tl_facade_stats->reply_stats;\n\n  send_time_cycles_ = base::CycleClock::Now();\n  PendingPin pin(send_time_cycles_);\n\n  pending_list.push_back(pin);\n\n  reply_stats.io_write_cnt++;\n  reply_stats.io_write_bytes += total_size_;\n  DVLOG(2) << \"Writing \" << total_size_ << \" bytes\";\n  if (auto ec = sink_->Write(vecs_.data(), vecs_.size()); ec)\n    ec_ = ec;\n\n  auto it = PendingList::s_iterator_to(pin);\n  pending_list.erase(it);\n\n  send_time_cycles_ = 0;\n\n  uint64_t after_cycles = base::CycleClock::Now();\n  reply_stats.send_stats.count++;\n  reply_stats.send_stats.total_duration += (after_cycles - pin.timestamp_cycles);\n  DVLOG(2) << \"Finished writing \" << total_size_ << \" bytes\";\n}\n\nvoid SinkReplyBuilder::FinishScope() {\n  replies_recorded_++;\n\n  if (!batched_ || total_size_ * 2 >= kMaxBufferSize /* copying isn't worth it */)\n    return Flush();\n\n  // Check if we have enough space to copy all refs to buffer\n  size_t ref_bytes = total_size_ - buffer_.InputLen();\n  if (ref_bytes > buffer_.AppendLen())\n    return Flush(ref_bytes);\n\n  // Copy all external references to buffer to safely keep batching\n  for (size_t i = guaranteed_pieces_; i < vecs_.size(); i++) {\n    auto ib = buffer_.InputBuffer();\n    if (vecs_[i].iov_base >= ib.data() && vecs_[i].iov_base <= ib.data() + ib.size())\n      continue;  // this is a piece\n\n    DCHECK_LE(vecs_[i].iov_len, buffer_.AppendLen());\n    void* dest = buffer_.AppendBuffer().data();\n    memcpy(dest, vecs_[i].iov_base, vecs_[i].iov_len);\n    buffer_.CommitWrite(vecs_[i].iov_len);\n    vecs_[i].iov_base = dest;\n  }\n  guaranteed_pieces_ = vecs_.size();  // all vecs are pieces\n}\n\nMCReplyBuilder::MCReplyBuilder(::io::Sink* sink) : SinkReplyBuilder(sink) {\n}\n\nvoid MCReplyBuilder::SendValue(MemcacheCmdFlags cmd_flags, std::string_view key,\n                               std::string_view value, uint64_t mc_token, uint32_t mc_flag,\n                               uint32_t ttl_sec) {\n  ReplyScope scope(this);\n  if (cmd_flags.meta) {\n    string flags;\n    if (cmd_flags.return_flags)\n      absl::StrAppend(&flags, \" f\", mc_flag);\n    if (cmd_flags.return_cas)\n      absl::StrAppend(&flags, \" c\", mc_token);\n    if (cmd_flags.return_ttl)\n      absl::StrAppend(&flags, \" t\", ttl_sec);\n\n    if (cmd_flags.return_value) {\n      WritePieces(\"VA \", value.size(), flags, kCRLF);\n      if (value.size() <= kMaxInlineSize) {\n        WritePieces(value, kCRLF);\n      } else {\n        WriteRef(value);\n        WritePieces(kCRLF);\n      }\n    } else {\n      WritePieces(\"HD \", flags, kCRLF);\n    }\n  } else {\n    WritePieces(\"VALUE \", key, \" \", mc_flag, \" \", value.size());\n    if (cmd_flags.return_cas)\n      WritePieces(\" \", mc_token);\n\n    if (value.size() <= kMaxInlineSize) {\n      WritePieces(kCRLF, value, kCRLF);\n    } else {","numLines":255,"startLine":30,"totalLines":520}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6adbe61d-f6b9-41af-9c62-a1fa05bf65c8","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}

Control Path: Admin events (Migrations, Checkpoints, PubSub) use a deque (dispatch_q_)\n1976\t//\n1977\t// AsyncFiber drains these queues according to system prioritization, ensuring\n1978\t// high-priority events are handled promptly while preventing priority inversion\n1979\t// during thread migrations. For simple requests, the InputLoop may bypass this\n1980\t// fiber and dispatch synchronously to minimize latency.\n1981\tvoid Connection::AsyncFiber() {\n1982\t  ThisFiber::SetName(\"AsyncFiber\");\n1983\t\n1984\t  AsyncOperations async_op{reply_builder_.get(), this};\n1985\t  size_t squashing_threshold = GetFlag(FLAGS_pipeline_squash);\n1986\t  uint64_t prev_epoch = fb2::FiberSwitchEpoch();\n1987\t  fb2::NoOpLock noop_lk;\n1988\t  QueueBackpressure& qbp = GetQueueBackpressure();\n1989\t  auto& conn_stats = tl_facade_stats->conn_stats;\n1990\t  uint32_t dispatch_q_cmd_processed = 0;\n1991\t  uint32_t async_dispatch_quota = GetFlag(FLAGS_async_dispatch_quota);\n1992\t\n1993\t  while (!reply_builder_->GetError()) {\n1994\t    DCHECK_EQ(socket()->proactor(), ProactorBase::me());\n1995\t    cnd_.wait(noop_lk, [this] {\n1996\t      if (cc_->conn_closing)\n1997\t        return true;\n1998\t\n1999\t      // If we are currently executing a synchronous dispatch (e.g. inside IoLoop),\n2000\t      // we must wait until it finishes to avoid race conditions.\n2001\t      if (cc_->sync_dispatch)\n2002\t        return false;\n2003\t\n2004\t      // For Memcache, we ONLY wake up for Admin messages (dispatch_q_) as we process\n2005\t      // parsed_head_  in the connection fiber. For RESP, we wake up for both queues.\n2006\t      if (protocol_ == Protocol::MEMCACHE) {\n2007\t        return !dispatch_q_.empty();\n2008\t      }\n2009\t      return HasPendingMessages();\n2010\t    });\n2011\t\n2012\t    if (cc_->conn_closing)\n2013\t      break;\n2014\t\n2015\t    // We really want to have batching in the builder if possible. This is especially\n2016\t    // critical in situations where Nagle's algorithm can introduce unwanted high\n2017\t    // latencies. However we can only batch if we're sure that there are more commands\n2018\t    // on the way that will trigger a flush. To know if there are, we sometimes yield before\n2019\t    // executing the last command in the queue and let the producer fiber push more commands if it\n2020\t    // wants to.\n2021\t    // As an optimization, we only yield if the fiber was not suspended since the last dispatch.\n2022\t    uint64_t cur_epoch = fb2::FiberSwitchEpoch();\n2023\t    if ((GetPendingMessageCount() == 1) && (cur_epoch == prev_epoch)) {\n2024\t      if (pipeline_wait_batch_usec > 0) {\n2025\t        ThisFiber::SleepFor(chrono::microseconds(pipeline_wait_batch_usec));\n2026\t      } else {\n2027\t        ThisFiber::Yield();\n2028\t      }\n2029\t      DVLOG(2) << \"After yielding to producer, parsed_cmd_q_len_=\" << parsed_cmd_q_len_\n2030\t               << \" dispatch_q size=\" << dispatch_q_.size();\n2031\t      if (cc_->conn_closing)\n2032\t        break;\n2033\t    }\n2034\t    prev_epoch = cur_epoch;\n2035\t\n2036\t    reply_builder_->SetBatchMode(GetPendingMessageCount() > 1);\n2037\t\n2038\t    bool subscriber_over_limit =\n2039\t        conn_stats.dispatch_queue_subscriber_bytes >= qbp.publish_buffer_limit;\n2040\t\n2041\t    // The below if/else conditionally choose between 3 message processing policies:\n2042\t    // 1. Pipeline squashing\n2043\t    // 2. Process pipeline queue\n2044\t    // 3. Process admin queue\n2045\t    //\n2046\t    // Special case: if the dispatch queue accumulated a big number of commands,\n2047\t    // we can try to squash them\n2048\t    // It is only enabled if the threshold is reached and the whole dispatch queue\n2049\t    // consists only of commands (no pubsub or monitor messages)\n2050\t    bool squashing_enabled = squashing_threshold > 0;\n2051\t    bool threshold_reached = parsed_cmd_q_len_ > squashing_threshold;\n2052\t    if (squashing_enabled && threshold_reached && dispatch_q_.empty() && !skip_next_squashing_ &&\n2053\t        !IsReplySizeOverLimit()) {  // 1. Pipeline squashing\n2054\t      SquashPipeline();"}]},"uuid":"e3db525f-96e5-4319-a92a-40b65cee49b9","timestamp":"2026-07-07T12:11:28.089Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/shared/Projects/dragonfly-src/src/facade/dragonfly_connection.cc","content":"// 2. Control Path: Admin events (Migrations, Checkpoints, PubSub) use a deque (dispatch_q_)\n//\n// AsyncFiber drains these queues according to system prioritization, ensuring\n// high-priority events are handled promptly while preventing priority inversion\n// during thread migrations. For simple requests, the InputLoop may bypass this\n// fiber and dispatch synchronously to minimize latency.\nvoid Connection::AsyncFiber() {\n  ThisFiber::SetName(\"AsyncFiber\");\n\n  AsyncOperations async_op{reply_builder_.get(), this};\n  size_t squashing_threshold = GetFlag(FLAGS_pipeline_squash);\n  uint64_t prev_epoch = fb2::FiberSwitchEpoch();\n  fb2::NoOpLock noop_lk;\n  QueueBackpressure& qbp = GetQueueBackpressure();\n  auto& conn_stats = tl_facade_stats->conn_stats;\n  uint32_t dispatch_q_cmd_processed = 0;\n  uint32_t async_dispatch_quota = GetFlag(FLAGS_async_dispatch_quota);\n\n  while (!reply_builder_->GetError()) {\n    DCHECK_EQ(socket()->proactor(), ProactorBase::me());\n    cnd_.wait(noop_lk, [this] {\n      if (cc_->conn_closing)\n        return true;\n\n      // If we are currently executing a synchronous dispatch (e.g. inside IoLoop),\n      // we must wait until it finishes to avoid race conditions.\n      if (cc_->sync_dispatch)\n        return false;\n\n      // For Memcache, we ONLY wake up for Admin messages (dispatch_q_) as we process\n      // parsed_head_  in the connection fiber. For RESP, we wake up for both queues.\n      if (protocol_ == Protocol::MEMCACHE) {\n        return !dispatch_q_.empty();\n      }\n      return HasPendingMessages();\n    });\n\n    if (cc_->conn_closing)\n      break;\n\n    // We really want to have batching in the builder if possible. This is especially\n    // critical in situations where Nagle's algorithm can introduce unwanted high\n    // latencies. However we can only batch if we're sure that there are more commands\n    // on the way that will trigger a flush. To know if there are, we sometimes yield before\n    // executing the last command in the queue and let the producer fiber push more commands if it\n    // wants to.\n    // As an optimization, we only yield if the fiber was not suspended since the last dispatch.\n    uint64_t cur_epoch = fb2::FiberSwitchEpoch();\n    if ((GetPendingMessageCount() == 1) && (cur_epoch == prev_epoch)) {\n      if (pipeline_wait_batch_usec > 0) {\n        ThisFiber::SleepFor(chrono::microseconds(pipeline_wait_batch_usec));\n      } else {\n        ThisFiber::Yield();\n      }\n      DVLOG(2) << \"After yielding to producer, parsed_cmd_q_len_=\" << parsed_cmd_q_len_\n               << \" dispatch_q size=\" << dispatch_q_.size();\n      if (cc_->conn_closing)\n        break;\n    }\n    prev_epoch = cur_epoch;\n\n    reply_builder_->SetBatchMode(GetPendingMessageCount() > 1);\n\n    bool subscriber_over_limit =\n        conn_stats.dispatch_queue_subscriber_bytes >= qbp.publish_buffer_limit;\n\n    // The below if/else conditionally choose between 3 message processing policies:\n    // 1. Pipeline squashing\n    // 2. Process pipeline queue\n    // 3. Process admin queue\n    //\n    // Special case: if the dispatch queue accumulated a big number of commands,\n    // we can try to squash them\n    // It is only enabled if the threshold is reached and the whole dispatch queue\n    // consists only of commands (no pubsub or monitor messages)\n    bool squashing_enabled = squashing_threshold > 0;\n    bool threshold_reached = parsed_cmd_q_len_ > squashing_threshold;\n    if (squashing_enabled && threshold_reached && dispatch_q_.empty() && !skip_next_squashing_ &&\n        !IsReplySizeOverLimit()) {  // 1. Pipeline squashing\n      SquashPipeline();","numLines":80,"startLine":1975,"totalLines":3422}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0202e7c4-2c3a-478f-9b36-6362f6d91122","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}
