Here is the complete report.\n\n---\n\n# Helio / Dragonfly io_uring — technical study (read-only)\n\nAll line numbers are as-of the trees on this box: `helio-src/util/fibers/` and `dragonfly-src/src/`.\n\n## 1. RING SETUP\n\nEverything is in `UringProactor::Init()` — `helio-src/util/fibers/uring_proactor.cc:159-277`. Setup is **kernel-version-gated, not runtime-probed with fallback**; if a flag combo is rejected the whole `io_uring_queue_init_params` fails and helio `LOG(FATAL)`s (`:216-228`). The comment at `:182-183` states the design: \"If we setup flags that kernel does not recognize, it fails the setup call.\"\n\nFlags actually set (`:183-207`):\n- kernel ≥ **5.19**: `IORING_SETUP_SUBMIT_ALL` (`:184`); also enables `poll_first_` (`:186`), buffer-ring support `buf_ring_f_` (`:189`), and optional direct-fd table (`:192`).\n- kernel ≥ **6.1**: `IORING_SETUP_DEFER_TASKRUN | IORING_SETUP_COOP_TASKRUN | IORING_SETUP_TASKRUN_FLAG | IORING_SETUP_SINGLE_ISSUER` all together (`:199-200`), and sets `taskrun_flag_f_=1`. Comment (`:197-198`): `TASKRUN_FLAG` is required so the kernel publishes `IORING_SQ_TASKRUN` in `sq_flags`, which the main loop reads to decide whether it must enter the kernel.\n- kernel ≥ **6.14** (margin over the official 6.12): `incremental_buf_ring_f_=1` → `IOU_PBUF_RING_INC` for incremental provided buffers (`:205-206`).\n- **SQPOLL**: not used — explicitly commented out at `:209-212` (\"SQPOLL requires registering each fd... not in short-term\").\n- **CQSIZE**: **not set.** No `IORING_SETUP_CQSIZE`, no `cq_entries` override anywhere (grep-confirmed). CQ defaults to 2× SQ entries; the boot log prints it (`:263-264`).\n\nRing sizing: `ring_size` is passed into `Init()` (must be power of two, ≥ 8; `:160-161`) and fed to `io_uring_queue_init_params(ring_size, &ring_, &params)` (`:216`). `centries_` (completion-callback slots) is sized to `params.sq_entries` (`:267`).\n\nregister_ring_fd: **yes** — `io_uring_register_ring_fd(&ring_)` at `:248`, but only if kernel ≥ 6 (`:247`), with a comment (`:244-246`) that on Oracle 5.15 it falsely succeeds and then deadlocks at unregister.\n\nProbing/feature checks: `GetKernelVersion` requires ≥ 5.8 (`:166-170`); `io_uring_get_probe_ring` checks `IORING_OP_MSG_RING` support → `msgring_supported_f_` (`:230-232`); required features `IORING_FEAT_SINGLE_MMAP | FAST_POLL | NODROP` are asserted (`:236-238`); `IORING_FEAT_RECVSEND_BUNDLE` sets `bundle_f_` (`:240-242`). Optional `io_uring_set_iowait(false)` behind `--uring_disable_iowait` (`:251-254`). Direct/fixed-file table is opt-in via `--uring_direct_table_len` (default 0 = off; `:27`, `:192`, `:257-259`).\n\n## 2. SUBMIT MODEL — the fiber↔CQE loop\n\n`UringProactor::MainLoop()` — `uring_proactor.cc:785-1029`. **One ring per proactor thread. SQEs are batched, not one-submit-per-op.** Fibers acquire SQEs lazily through `GetSubmitEntry()` (`:367-401`), which calls `io_uring_get_sqe` and only submits eagerly if the SQ ring is *full* (`:369-377`). Many fibers thus queue many SQEs that flush together.\n\nPer loop pass:\n1. **Decide whether to enter the kernel** (`:829-836`): under `taskrun_flag_f_`, compute `call_submit = io_uring_sq_ready() > 0 || (sq.kflags & IORING_SQ_TASKRUN)`. So it skips the syscall entirely when there is nothing to submit and no deferred task-work pending.\n2. If entering, call **`io_uring_submit_and_get_events(&ring_)`** (`:835`) — submit + drain in ONE `io_uring_enter` with `GETEVENTS` always set. The comment at `:821-826` is the crux: under `DEFER_TASKRUN`, plain `io_uring_submit` does *not* set `GETEVENTS` unless `SQ_TASKRUN` happens to be observed, so it can submit without draining completions and add latency — hence they always use the `_and_get_events` variant.\n3. Peek completions in a batch: `io_uring_peek_batch_cqe(&ring_, cqes, kCqeBatchLen=128)` (`:881`), then `ReapCompletions` (`:347-365`) loops `ProcessCqeBatch` + `io_uring_cq_advance` draining 128 at a time. Each CQE dispatches through `centries_[user_data]` to the stored callback (`ProcessCqeBatch`, `:279-345`); for `FiberCall` that callback stores the result and `ActivateSameThread`s the waiting fiber (`:1084-1091`).\n4. `scheduler->Run(NORMAL)` runs the now-ready fibers (`:896`); those may issue more SQEs, then `continue` restarts the pass so they get submitted.\n5. Only when task queue empty, no ready fibers, no pending SQEs, on-idle done, and busy-poll budget exhausted (`Pause(3)` spin, `:955-963`) does it actually **block**: `wait_for_cqe(&ring_, 1, ts_arg)` = `io_uring_wait_cqes(ring, &cqe, wait_nr=1, ts)` (`:49-56`, `:1011`), guarded by a `tq_seq_` CAS into `WAIT_SECTION_STATE` (`:999-1028`) so cross-thread wakers know to wake it.\n\nSo: the hot path is **batched submit_and_get_events (non-blocking drain) once per pass**; the blocking single-CQE wait is a *separate* idle-only call. This is the \"fully-exploited\" shape — the enter is skipped when idle-clean, and each enter both flushes the batch and reaps.\n\n`FiberCall` (`:1083-1108`) is the per-op primitive: grab an SQE with a waker callback, optionally chain a link-timeout (`IOSQE_IO_LINK` + `PrepLinkTimeout`, `:1098-1107`), then `me->Suspend()` in `Get()`.\n\n## 3. RECV PATH\n\nTwo modes, in `uring_socket.cc`:\n\n- **Default = synchronous per-recv** (this is what Dragonfly uses out of the box). `UringSocket::Recv` (`:353-390`) / `RecvMsg` (`:308-351`): one `PrepRecv`/`PrepRecvMsg` SQE per call into the caller's own buffer, via `FiberCall`. It sets `IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST` **conditionally** — only when `has_pollfirst_ && !has_recv_data_` (`:327-329`, `:364-366`) — i.e. skip the speculative inline recv attempt unless we already know bytes are queued. It reads back `IORING_CQE_F_SOCK_NONEMPTY` into `has_recv_data_` (`:370`) to drive that decision next time.\n- **Multishot + provided-buffer ring = opt-in.** Enabled per-socket by `EnableRecvMultishot()` and only if a buf-ring is registered (`RegisterOnRecv`, `:455-539`). The arm: `PrepRecv(fd, nullptr, 0, 0)` with `IOSQE_BUFFER_SELECT`, `sqe.buf_group = bufring_id_` (`:519-524`), `ioprio |= IORING_RECV_MULTISHOT | IORING_RECVSEND_POLL_FIRST` (`:525`, POLL_FIRST **always** here), plus `IORING_RECVSEND_BUNDLE` if supported (`:527-529`). Completion (`:464-517`) reads `IORING_CQE_F_BUFFER`, walks segments through `BufRingGetBufPtr`/`BufRingTrackRecvCompletion` (bundle + incremental aware), delivers each as `RecvNotification{MutableBytes}`. On `!IORING_CQE_F_MORE` (kernel stopped multishot, typically buffer exhaustion) it re-registers (`:486-492`). `ENOBUFS` is currently an unhandled `LOG(FATAL)` TODO (`:499-504`).\n\nBuffer ring: `RegisterBufferRing` (`uring_proactor.cc:463-512`) via `io_uring_setup_buf_ring` (`IOU_PBUF_RING_INC` when incremental supported). Recycling = `AdvanceHead()` (`:96-101`) → `io_uring_buf_ring_add` + `io_uring_buf_ring_advance`, returning the entry to the kernel; incremental buffers reuse a partially-filled 1500B entry via `incr_offset` rather than burning a whole entry per small datagram (`:528-551`).\n\nIn Dragonfly the buf-ring is **off by default**: `--uring_recv_buffer_cnt` defaults to **0** (`server/dfly_main.cc:122`, `RegisterBufRings` at `:776-805`), group id `kRecvSockGid=0`, entry size `kRecvBufSize=1500` (MTU-sized) (`facade/facade_types.h:277,280`), requires kernel ≥ 6.2, rounded up to a power of two. `dragonfly_connection.cc:3151-3157` (`MaybeEnableRecvMultishot`) only flips multishot on when `BufRingEntrySize(kRecvSockGid) > 0 && !is_tls_`. So Dragonfly's *default* recv is the synchronous `PrepRecv` into the connection's `io_buf_`.\n\n## 4. SEND PATH\n\n**Plain `SEND`/`SENDMSG` only. There is no zero-copy send anywhere in helio or Dragonfly** (grep for `SEND_ZC`/`send_zc`/`F_NOTIF`/`prep_send_zc` across both trees = zero hits). `UringSocket::WriteSome` (`uring_socket.cc:180-240`): `len==1` → `PrepSend` (`IORING_OP_SEND`) with `MSG_NOSIGNAL`; `len>1` → `PrepSendMsg` (`IORING_OP_SENDMSG`). `AsyncWriteSome` (`:242-274`) → async `PrepSendMsg` with a heap `msghdr` + completion callback. No `F_NOTIF` handling, no registered buffers on the send path. The proactor *has* a generic registered-buffer pool (`RegisterBuffers`/`RequestRegisteredSlice`, `uring_proactor.cc:403-461`) but it is **not** wired into socket send (it exists for file/other I/O). Dragonfly's reply path bottoms out at `sink_->Write(vecs_.data(), vecs_.size())` (`facade/reply_builder.cc:210`) → `WriteSome` → SENDMSG. So helio leaves ZC-send entirely on the table.\n\n## 5. ACCEPT / connection assignment\n\n**No multishot accept.** `UringSocket::Accept` (`uring_socket.cc:106-147`) is a loop of blocking `accept4(SOCK_NONBLOCK|SOCK_CLOEXEC)`; on `EAGAIN` it arms a `FiberCall` `PrepPollAdd(POLLIN)` and retries. A single `AcceptLoop` fiber (`listener_interface.cc:96-165`, `RunAcceptLoop`) runs on the listener's proactor and blocking-accepts.\n\nAssignment: `Listener::PickConnectionProactor` (`dragonfly_listener.cc:330-384`) picks the target thread via `SO_INCOMING_CPU` (+`SO_INCOMING_NAPI_ID`) when `--conn_use_incoming_cpu` is set (`:345-368`) so the connection lands on the proactor whose thread runs on the NIC's RX CPU; otherwise round-robins `next_id_.fetch_add(1) % conn_io_threads` (`:372-381`). The accepted socket is then **migrated** onto that proactor (`SetProactor`/`ListenerInterface::Migrate`, `:350-415`), so all subsequent socket ops issue on the owning thread — single-issuer stays intact.\n\n## 6. THREADING\n\nStrictly **one ring per proactor thread, single-issuer**. Sockets assert `ProactorBase::me() == proactor` on every op (`uring_socket.cc:190`, `:316`, `:356`). Cross-thread wakeup = `WakeRing` (`uring_proactor.cc:1062-1081`): on kernels with `IORING_OP_MSG_RING` support, **the caller proactor submits a `MSG_RING` SQE on its *own* ring targeting the destination ring's `ring_fd`** (`PrepMsgRing(ring_.ring_fd,0,0)`, `:1075-1076`) — this is single-issuer-legal because each proactor only ever submits to its own ring; the kernel delivers the completion into the target. Fallback on older kernels = `write(wake_fd_, ...)` eventfd (`:1079`) with an armed `POLL_ADD` on `wake_fd_` (`ArmWakeupEvent`, `:667-684`). The `tq_seq_`/`WAIT_SECTION_STATE` protocol ensures exactly one waker fires. **Helio never issues a ring op on another thread's ring** — migration physically moves the socket first, so even cancels run same-thread (`ResetOnRecvHook` → `CancelRequests`/`io_uring_register_sync_cancel` on the owning proactor, `:541-552`).\n\n## 7. Notable / clever\n\n- `IOSQE_IO_LINK` for link-timeouts (`FiberCall`, `:1098-1107`) and for the wakeup poll+read pair (`ArmWakeupEvent:674`).\n- `MSG_RING` cross-ring wakeups (avoids eventfd syscall) — the standout multi-thread trick.\n- Multishot `POLL_ADD` (`IORING_POLL_ADD_MULTI`) used to emulate epoll (`EpollAdd`/`RearmEpoll`, `:578-617`) and for error-cb `POLLERR|POLLHUP` (`RegisterOnErrorCb`, `uring_socket.cc:392-423`).\n- `IORING_SETUP_SUBMIT_ALL` so one bad SQE doesn't abort the whole batch.\n- Incremental provided-buffer rings (`IOU_PBUF_RING_INC`) + `RECVSEND_BUNDLE` for efficient small-recv into MTU buffers.\n- Direct/fixed descriptors exist (`register_fds_`, `IOSQE_FIXED_FILE`) but are **disabled for sockets** — `kEnableDirect=false` (`uring_socket.cc:26`), citing liburing issue #1192.\n- Busy-poll `Pause(3)` spin + optional `io_uring_set_iowait(false)` to cut enter frequency / iowait accounting.\n- **NAPI busy-poll is NOT used** — no `io_uring_register_napi` anywhere; `SO_INCOMING_NAPI_ID` is only read for steering/logging.\n\n---\n\n## COMPARISON TABLE — helio/Dragonfly vs THredis `2s-deep-uring-dev` (commits `5c77d7b32`, `4315accef`)\n\n| Dimension | helio / Dragonfly | THredis deep-uring | Verdict |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **Setup flags** | `SINGLE_ISSUER\\|DEFER_TASKRUN\\|COOP_TASKRUN\\|TASKRUN_FLAG\\|SUBMIT_ALL`, kernel-version-gated, `LOG(FATAL)` if refused (no fallback) | `SINGLE_ISSUER\\|DEFER_TASKRUN`, runtime probe + **fallback to plain ring**; boot line names mode | **Match on SI\\|DEFER; we add fallback (more robust).** We appear to omit `COOP_TASKRUN`, `TASKRUN_FLAG`, `SUBMIT_ALL`. `TASKRUN_FLAG` is worth adopting — it's exactly what lets helio's loop skip the enter when clean (see submit model); `SUBMIT_ALL` matters since we *abort the whole pass* on submit failure. |\n| **register_ring_fd** | Yes (kernel ≥ 6) | Yes, after every successful setup | **Match.** |\n| **CQ sizing** | Default 2× SQ; `CQSIZE` never set | `IORING_SETUP_CQSIZE = max(depth, 2·nbufs)` | **We do more than helio** — justified: our multishot buf-ring can post a burst of per-buffer CQEs. Helio's MTU buf-ring is smaller/opt-in so it gets away with the default. Applicable and correct for us. |\n| **Submit model** | One `submit_and_get_events` per pass, gated by `sq_ready>0 \\|\\| SQ_TASKRUN`; separate blocking `wait_cqe(…,1)` only at idle | One `submit_and_wait(ring,1)` per `beforeSleep` pass | **Match in spirit (batch once/pass).** Difference: helio splits \"drain\" (non-blocking `submit_and_get_events` in the hot pass) from \"block\" (idle-only `wait_cqe`); we fuse submit+wait because our fiber-free `beforeSleep` synchronously consumes send *results* (plain-send buffer reuse depends on it). Their `SQ_TASKRUN` skip-the-enter test is a cheap win we could copy to avoid empty enters. |\n| **Recv** | Default synchronous `PrepRecv` + conditional POLL_FIRST; multishot+buf-ring **opt-in** (`--uring_recv_buffer_cnt`, default off), MTU (1500B) entries, **+ bundles + incremental buffers** | Runtime-sized provided-buffer ring + **always-on POLL_FIRST** multishot recv, ENOBUFS re-arm audited | **Match on the core (buf-ring, POLL_FIRST, ENOBUFS re-arm).** We are missing `IORING_RECVSEND_BUNDLE` and `IOU_PBUF_RING_INC` (incremental buffers). On a real NIC with many small requests these cut per-recv buffer waste and CQE count — **applicable to us and a concrete gap.** Also helio deliberately sizes recv entries at MTU; worth checking our entry size vs their 1500. |\n| **Send** | **Plain SEND/SENDMSG, no ZC, no registered send buffers** | **detach-on-submit `SEND_ZC` / `SEND_ZC_FIXED`, async F_NOTIF reaping, registered sparse buffer pool (128×16K/thread), gated >1 KiB** | **We do what helio does NOT.** This is our biggest divergence and matches the VLDB'26 \"exploited send = ZC>1KiB + regbufs\" guidance. On real-NIC large-value GET we should beat helio's copy-based send. Helio's tradeoff is simplicity (buffer reuse right after the synchronously-consumed completion). Note: on **loopback** ZC-send is typically neutral-to-negative (kernel still copies), so this only pays off on a real NIC — consistent with our \"loopback = neutral\" expectation. |\n| **Registered buffers (send)** | Generic pool exists but **not wired to socket send** | `register_buffers_sparse` + `update_tag` + `SEND_ZC_FIXED` | **We exploit it; helio doesn't.** |\n| **Accept** | Single AcceptLoop, `accept4`+`PollAdd`, **no multishot accept**; assign via `SO_INCOMING_CPU`/NAPI or round-robin, then migrate | Dedicated IO threads own connections | **Neither uses multishot accept.** Helio's `SO_INCOMING_CPU`/NAPI-id steering (place conn on the NIC-RX CPU) is a real-NIC locality win we do not appear to do — **worth considering for the Threadripper/25GbE eval** (multi-CCD makes RX-CPU locality matter). |\n| **Threading / cross-thread wake** | One ring/thread, single-issuer; wake via **MSG_RING submitted on caller's own ring** (or eventfd); **never issues a ring op on another thread's ring** — migrates the socket first so even cancels are same-thread | Dedicated IO threads + fiber-free; **recv cancel is submitted from the MAIN thread** under `pauseIOThread`, which is cross-task → SI\\|DTR returns `-EINVAL` → **recv ring forced to stay PLAIN** | **Architecture-driven divergence, not a miss.** Helio sidesteps our exact `-EINVAL` problem by *physically migrating the socket to the target thread* so the cancel runs same-thread on an SI\\|DTR ring. Our main-thread-driven migration/cancel model is what forces the plain recv ring. If we could route the disarm-cancel to run *on* the owning IO thread (helio-style), our recv ring could also be SI\\|DTR — but that reopens the W6-U2 byte-loss window under our model. Correct call given our threading; just note it's the reason, not a limitation of io_uring. |\n| **Direct/fixed FDs** | Machinery present, **disabled for sockets** (liburing #1192) | Not used | **Match (both off).** |\n| **NAPI busy-poll** | **Not used** | Not used | **Both miss it.** A real-NIC opportunity for both (`io_uring_register_napi`); flag for the Threadripper eval. |\n| **Linked SQEs** | `IOSQE_IO_LINK` for link-timeouts + wakeup poll/read | Not noted | Minor; not load-bearing for us. |\n\n**Net:** we match or exceed helio on setup hygiene (fallback probe, CQSIZE), match the submit-once-per-pass batching, match multishot-recv-with-POLL_FIRST, and go **beyond** helio on the send path (ZC + registered buffers — the thing helio leaves unexploited). The concrete things helio does that we don't and that **would matter on a real NIC**: (1) `RECVSEND_BUNDLE` + incremental provided buffers on recv; (2) `SO_INCOMING_CPU`/NAPI-id connection steering; (3) the `SQ_TASKRUN` skip-the-enter micro-opt; (4) `SUBMIT_ALL`/`TASKRUN_FLAG` in the flag set. None are blocked by our sharded-KV / dedicated-IO-thread / fiber-free model — they're all adoptable.\n\n---\n\n## ADDITIONAL SECTION — Dragonfly's large-value (≥256B) pipelined-GET collapse: source root cause\n\nThe collapse is **above the io_uring layer** — it is in Dragonfly's reply builder + squashing, not in `uring_socket`. The io_uring send path imposes no per-reply cap (question 2 answer: **no provided-buffer size cap on send, no batched→per-reply fallback in `uring_socket`** — send is just SENDMSG of whatever iovec array the reply builder hands it). Two compounding mechanisms in `facade/`:\n\n### Mechanism A — the 8 KB reply-coalescing cap + 32 B inline threshold (fundamental, compile-time)\n\nConstants in `facade/reply_builder.h:30-31`:\n```\nconstexpr static size_t kMaxInlineSize = 32;     // value copied inline vs sent by reference\nconstexpr static size_t kMaxBufferSize = 8192;   // coalescing scratch cap\n```\n`RedisReplyBuilderBase::SendBulkString` (`reply_builder.cc:336-344`):\n```\nif (str.size() <= kMaxInlineSize)                       // ≤32B: whole reply copied into buffer_\n  return WritePieces(kLengthPrefix, uint32_t(str.size()), kCRLF, str, kCRLF);\nWritePieces(kLengthPrefix, uint32_t(str.size()), kCRLF); // >32B: header inline...\nWriteRef(str);                                           // ...value as an EXTERNAL iovec (no copy)\nWritePieces(kCRLF);\n```\n`WriteRef` adds the value's bytes to `total_size_` (`:167`). The batch is flushed by `FinishScope` (runs at the end of every reply scope, `:224-247`):\n```\nif (!batched_ || total_size_ * 2 >= kMaxBufferSize /* copying isn't worth it */)\n  return Flush();                                        // total_size_ >= 4096 → SEND now\nsize_t ref_bytes = total_size_ - buffer_.InputLen();\nif (ref_bytes > buffer_.AppendLen())\n  return Flush(ref_bytes);\n// else memcpy every external ref into buffer_ to keep coalescing (capped at kMaxBufferSize)\n```\nConsequence, at fixed client pipeline depth:\n- **≤32 B values**: each reply is copied inline into the single contiguous `buffer_`; `ref_bytes≈0`, so it keeps coalescing until `total_size_ ≥ 4 KB` → ~**128 replies per send**, emitted as one `IORING_OP_SEND` (single iovec). One syscall amortized over ~128 ops.\n- **≥256 B / 512 B values**: value goes out as an external `WriteRef` iovec; `FinishScope` either copies it back into the ≤8 KB scratch or flushes, and the `total_size_*2 ≥ 8192` test forces a **Flush every ~8 replies (~4 KB)**, emitted as multi-iovec `IORING_OP_SENDMSG`. So a deep pipeline fragments into ~16× more send operations than the 32 B case, regardless of how deep the client pipelined. Send-rate becomes the bottleneck → the observed 3–6× throughput drop.\n\n`kMaxBufferSize`/`kMaxInlineSize` are `constexpr` (grep-confirmed: only defined in `reply_builder.h`, no flag) → **this cap is fundamental to the current build; raising it needs a recompile.** `--force_epoll` does not touch it (it's above the socket).\n\n### Mechanism B — default pipeline squashing adds a capture/replay + borrow-pin layer (tunable)\n\n`--pipeline_squash` defaults to **1** (`dragonfly_connection.cc:88`), so any pipeline deeper than 1 command is squashed: `AsyncFiber` (`:1985`, `:2050-2054`) takes the squash branch when `parsed_cmd_q_len_ > squashing_threshold`. Squashing runs each command against a `CapturingReplyBuilder` (`multi_command_squasher.cc:205`), **captures every reply into a `Payload` variant**, accumulates the whole unit, then `CapturingReplyBuilder::Apply` replays them into the real builder (`:351`).\n\nGood news for plain string GET: the borrowable path uses `SendBulkStringBorrowed(std::move(...))` (`string_family.cc:712`) → `Capture(std::move(bs))` (`reply_capture.cc:59-61`) which does **not** copy the value — it pins the `CompactObj` across the squash. But: (a) the non-borrowable fallbacks (`string_family.cc:730,734`) use `SendBulkString(str)` → `Capture(BulkString{string{str}})` (`reply_capture.cc:52-54`) which **full-copies every value into a heap std::string**; and (b) even on the borrowed path, squashing holds a borrow-pin on every value for the whole unit and then funnels all of them through the same Mechanism-A ≤8 KB coalescing at replay — so the send fragmentation still bites, now with added per-reply capture/variant/borrow bookkeeping. At 32 B this overhead is hidden by the shard-parallelism win; at ≥256 B the per-value cost dominates.\n\nThe squash size gate `--squashed_reply_size_limit` defaults to **0 = unlimited** (`dragonfly_connection.cc:112`), so squashing does **not** self-disable on large accumulated replies by default (`IsReplySizeOverLimit`, `:2574-2588` returns false when limit==0).\n\n### Config levers that mitigate / fix it\n\n- **`--pipeline_squash=0`** — removes Mechanism B entirely (individual dispatch, zero-copy `WriteRef`/borrowed replies, no capture/replay). Biggest single lever for large-value GET-heavy pipelines. *Tunable, runtime.*\n- **`--squashed_reply_size_limit=<bytes>`** (e.g. a few KB) — makes squashing fall back to single-command dispatch once accumulated reply size exceeds it (`:2053`, `:2574`). Targeted fix for exactly this large-value case. *Tunable.*\n- **`kMaxBufferSize` / `kMaxInlineSize`** (`reply_builder.h:30-31`) — raising these widens the coalescing window / inlines larger values, directly addressing Mechanism A, but requires **recompiling** (no flag). *Fundamental in current builds.*\n- **`--always_flush_pipeline`** flushes *after* each squash unit — makes it worse, not better.\n- **`--force_epoll`** — no effect; the collapse is in the reply builder, above the proactor.\n\n### Bottom line\n\nThe ≥256 B pipelined-GET collapse is **two-layered**: a *fundamental* compile-time coalescing cap (`kMaxBufferSize=8192`, `kMaxInlineSize=32` in `reply_builder.h`) that fragments large-value pipelines into ~16× more `SENDMSG`s once values exceed the 32 B inline threshold, plus a *tunable* default squashing layer (`pipeline_squash=1`) that adds capture/replay + borrow-pin overhead which is negligible at 32 B but dominant at 512 B. It reproduces because both effects are keyed on value size crossing the 32 B inline boundary. Runtime mitigation exists (`--pipeline_squash=0`, `--squashed_reply_size_limit`), but the send-fragmentation cap itself is baked into the build. For our comparison: THredis's ZC/registered-buffer send path deliberately keeps large values as one detached DMA rather than fragmenting them, so this is precisely a large-value regime where our design should pull ahead of Dragonfly on a real NIC.\n\nKey files: `/shared/Projects/dragonfly-src/src/facade/reply_builder.cc` (`:103-247` batching, `:336-344` bulk string), `/shared/Projects/dragonfly-src/src/facade/reply_builder.h` (`:30-31` constants), `/shared/Projects/dragonfly-src/src/facade/dragonfly_connection.cc` (`:88,112` flags, `:1981-2054` squash decision, `:2574-2588` reply-size gate), `/shared/Projects/dragonfly-src/src/facade/reply_capture.cc` (`:52-61` capture copy vs borrow), `/shared/Projects/dragonfly-src/src/server/multi_command_squasher.cc` (`:39,205,351` capture/replay)."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4072,"cache_read_input_tokens":120372,"output_tokens":14853,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4072},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":14853,"cache_read_input_tokens":120372,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4072,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4072,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CcngB8vwrjxPJLijyec2V","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"966c1072-194a-4e7b-9d0e-bb537e5c8211","timestamp":"2026-07-07T12:15:50.118Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/shared/Projects/THredis-2s-during/src","sessionId":"192d33d7-f025-4e9c-82b2-54335e52614f","version":"2.1.195","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"wondrous-dreaming-cook"}
